# SmartKeyNet — Session Log

> **Every person updates this file at the end of every Claude Code session, before pushing.**
> Format: copy the template below, fill it in, paste it at the TOP of this file (newest first).
> Commit message: `log: [Person X] [area] [date]`
> This file is the team's shared brain — if it's not logged, it didn't happen.

---

## Log Template (copy this, fill in, paste at top)

```
### [PERSON A/B/C/D] — [area] — [DATE] — [branch name]

**Session goal:** one sentence — what you set out to do.
**What got done:**
- bullet per file created or meaningfully changed
- include the function/class name if it matters

**What's working:** one sentence on current state of your area.
**What's broken / incomplete:** be honest — what didn't get done, what's failing.
**Blockers:** anything you need from another person before your next session.
**Next session will:** what you plan to do next time you open Claude Code.
**Hard Rules check:** did anything tempt you to violate a Hard Rule? How did you handle it?
```

## Sessions (newest first)

### [SOLO — render Living System panel from real tenant-graph episode data (static snapshots, real node identities)] — 2026-08-29 — main

**Branch sync confirmed before starting:** `git rev-parse main dev21` both `1b2b498805950ffda8ecdcd46b5805108050836e`.

**Session goal:** the review/demo is the near-term priority; this is the fourth of seven dashboard panels and the most visually impactful remaining one -- a visual of the real tenant graph with recent decisions colored by served key tier. Scope discipline per instruction: STATIC SNAPSHOTS of real decisions on the real tenant graph, not a live-animating/streaming/JS-driven view.

**Read first:** `PROGRESS.md` (confirmed the tenant graph and three prior rendered panels' status); `SESSION_LOG.md`'s Explain Decision + dose-response/S3-table entries (established rendering conventions: self-contained static HTML, inline SVG, zero heavy JS deps, no server, saved to `dashboard/samples/`); `env/request_generator.py` in full (`build_tenant_graph`'s real hub-and-spoke topology, `kms_hub` + `tenant_i` nodes, real persistent `sensitivity_class`/`traffic_rate`/`pqc_capable`/`services` node attrs -- confirmed these are generic `tenant_0..tenant_N` identities, never the mockup's hospital/fintech/logging/iot-telemetry labels); `dashboard/explain.py` (confirmed its `DecisionTrace` doesn't itself carry a resolved served-tier value -- see design decision below); `env/environment.py` (confirmed a real per-decision tenant identity exists via `env._current_request["tenant"]`/`["service"]`, and confirmed `_resulting_key_type(action, session, floor)` -- the exact ground-truth function `_apply_action` itself uses -- is the correct real source for "what tier does this decision actually serve," including `REUSE`/`REKEY_NOW`'s resolution, not just the three tier-serving `Action` values read directly); `dashboard/mockups/smartkeynet_dashboard_mockup_v2.html`'s Living System tab (styling reference only: hub-and-spoke SVG layout, tier color convention -- grey/classical `#8B95A5`, amber/pqc `#E8A33D`, green/hybrid `#33D687`, matching `render_explain.py`'s own CSS tokens -- recent-decisions list, side gauges; its 6 hand-placed node coordinates and hospital/fintech/etc. tenant names are 100% fabricated and were not read for data).

**Design decisions:**
- **Served-tier resolution reuses `SmartKeyNetEnv._resulting_key_type` directly** rather than re-deriving "what tier did this decision serve" from the chosen `Action` alone -- `REUSE` serves whatever tier the existing session already has, and `REKEY_NOW` resolves to `max(existing tier, floor)`, neither of which is recoverable from the action value alone. Reusing the environment's own real resolution function (read-only, same established precedent as `dashboard/explain.py` reaching into `env._current_request`/`env._pool_sim`) means the panel's colors can never drift from what the environment itself actually did.
- **Static-snapshot approach, not live/animated**: `dashboard/render_living_system.py::build_snapshot()` folds a real decision history up to a real ordinal cutoff into one frozen `LivingSystemSnapshot` (real per-tenant "most recently served tier," or `None` -- shown honestly as "no traffic yet," never defaulted to a tier); `render_living_system_html()` renders exactly one snapshot to one self-contained HTML/SVG file. Three such snapshots are rendered per episode (see below), not one continuously-updating view -- exactly the brief's "clear, truthful picture... not a real-time simulation."
- **Graph layout**: inline SVG, hub at a fixed center point, tenant nodes placed on a ring via `2*pi*i/n` -- a real, deterministic layout of however many real tenant nodes `build_tenant_graph` actually returned (tested across `n_nodes` in {3,7,10}), never the mockup's fixed 6-node layout.
- **Scenario/policy choice: S2 (HNDL posture), `StaticThresholdPolicy(0.5)`** (same established baseline `render_explain_demo.py` uses) -- S1's floor never moves, so its snapshots would look nearly identical; S2 is the one scenario whose real threat/posture is designed to change mid-episode, argued as the strongest "system is alive" story.
- **Snapshot selection redesigned mid-session after a real, honestly-investigated finding (Hard Rule 7)**: the original plan was "early/calm" -> "ratchet fires around S2's scripted `elevate_at_step=50`" -> "late/settled." Instrumented and checked directly: across seeds 0-3, `PolicyTable`'s one-way ratchet actually fires within the first 1-2 real decisions of the episode -- 40-50 decisions *before* `elevate_at_step`. Traced to the real mechanism: `env/environment.py::_threat_features_placeholder`'s ordinary (pre-elevation) signal is `[qber, load]`, and `load` (queue backlog / 10) climbs fast enough under this graph's real per-tenant arrival pattern that `MovingAverageForecaster`'s `sigmoid(mean([qber, load]))` EWMA crosses into ELEVATED-classified territory almost immediately -- a property of the placeholder threat-feature formula mixing an ordinary congestion metric into what's meant to represent threat (verified this reproduces with `random_request_generator` too, not something specific to the graph). Once ratcheted, posture never lowers (Hard Rule 2), so for this real run S2's scripted schedule is superseded by an earlier organic escalation. **Not a bug in this session's own code, not fixed here** (would touch `env/forecast_provider.py`, needs sign-off -- flagged below and in PROGRESS.md, cross-referencing the standing `env/forecast_provider.py` posture-ceiling open item). Snapshot selection was redesigned around real milestones instead of the (unreliable) schedule-timing assumption: first decision, first decision by which every real tenant has been served at least once (found by scanning, not assumed), and the final decision.
- **Tier -> color mapping**: `KeyType.CLASSICAL/PQC/HYBRID` -> the exact same hex values already established in `render_explain.py`'s CSS tokens (`#8B95A5`/`#E8A33D`/`#33D687`), matching the mockup's own convention. A tenant with no real decision yet in the snapshot's window renders with a distinct fourth color (`#4C5A6B`, "no traffic yet") -- never defaulted to a tier.

**What got done:**
- `dashboard/render_living_system.py` (new): `TenantNodeView`/`RecentDecisionView`/`LivingSystemSnapshot` dataclasses; `build_snapshot()` (pure fold over a real decision history + real graph node attrs into one frozen snapshot, ordinal-cutoff-based -- not step-number-based, since two decisions can share an internal tick); `render_living_system_html()` (pure view: real tenant ring layout + tier-colored edges/nodes + real recent-decisions list + real pool_fill/posture side panel); `write_living_system_html()`.
- `dashboard/render_living_system_demo.py` (new): `collect_real_episode()` builds the real graph (`build_tenant_graph(n_nodes=10, seed=7)` -- a dedicated structural seed decoupled from the episode seed, same rationale as S4/S6's own `graph_seed`/`migration_graph_seed`), runs a real 250-decision S2 episode via `request_stream_factory` injection (the same swap-test precedent `tests/test_environment.py` already exercises), and for each decision reads the real tenant/service/floor/session-key-state to compute the real served tier via `env._resulting_key_type`. `pick_snapshot_indices()` selects the three real milestone indices described above. `main()` writes `dashboard/samples/living_system_01_first_decision.html`, `_02_graph_fully_populated.html`, `_03_final_decision.html`.
- **A real naming collision caught before it reached a committed state**: this driver's first snapshot was originally named `01_first_decision.html`, silently clobbering `render_explain_demo.py`'s own real sample of the identical name in the shared `dashboard/samples/` directory (`git status`/`git diff --stat` surfaced it as a modified, not new, file after the first run). Restored the clobbered file via `git checkout --` and renamed this session's outputs to a `living_system_`-prefixed set before anything was committed -- flagging here since `dashboard/samples/` is a shared output directory across every panel's demo driver and this is worth remembering for whoever builds the next one.
- **A real bug caught by a test before shipping**: the first render attempt used `tenant.last_served_tier.name if tenant.last_served_tier else "NONE"` for the SVG's `data-tier` attribute -- `KeyType.CLASSICAL == 0` is a falsy `IntEnum` value, so this silently rendered every CLASSICAL-served tenant as "no traffic yet" instead of its real tier. Caught by `test_tier_color_mapping_is_exact`'s exact-color assertion (parametrized across all three real tiers), fixed to `is not None`, re-verified. Did not appear in the actual saved demo samples only because this particular real run never happened to serve CLASSICAL (S2's near-immediate HIGH posture makes `StaticThresholdPolicy` favor `SERVE_HYBRID` almost throughout) -- a latent bug regardless, worth the dedicated per-tier test existing at all.
- `tests/test_render_living_system.py` (new, 17 tests, all against the real `build_tenant_graph`, never a hand-built `nx.Graph`): `build_snapshot` correctness (most-recent-tier-wins fold, ordinal- not step-cutoff, recent-decisions window/order, rejects an empty cutoff); real node-count/topology tests parametrized across `n_nodes` in {3,7,10} (asserts rendered tenant-node count and edge count both equal the real `n_nodes`, never a fixed number); the real hub node id appears; **tier-color correctness**, parametrized across all three real tiers, asserting the exact mapped hex color on both the node and its edge (this is the test that caught the CLASSICAL bug above); the "no traffic yet" color/label for an untouched real tenant; a no-fabrication check (every rendered tenant id/tier is a member of the real input, and the mockup's fabricated tenant names never appear); real `pool_fill`/posture shown verbatim; HTML/SVG well-formedness (the same balanced-tag `HTMLParser` pattern established by prior sessions, including an edge case with exactly one recent decision); a `write_living_system_html` round-trip test.
- Full `pytest` suite: **614 passed, 1 xfailed** (597 prior + 17 new), ~30s.

**What's working:** the Living System panel renders the real tenant graph's real node count/topology/attributes with tier colors verified exact per-tier by dedicated tests; the demo driver produces 3 genuinely different real snapshots from one real S2 episode, honestly re-argued around what the real data actually showed rather than the originally-assumed schedule-driven story.
**What's broken / incomplete:** the remaining three mockup-only dashboard panels (Budgeting Brain, Migration Wave, Threat Input) are unchanged, deliberately out of scope this session. The `env/forecast_provider.py` placeholder threat-feature formula's `load`-sensitivity (this session's finding: it ratchets posture up almost immediately under ordinary traffic, independent of any scripted schedule) is flagged, not fixed -- related to, but distinct from, the standing posture-*ceiling* item already in PROGRESS.md; both point at the same placeholder formula needing a future calibration pass.
**Blockers:** none.
**Next session will:** per updated PROGRESS.md -- pick up one of the remaining three mockup-only panels (Budgeting Brain, Migration Wave, Threat Input -- the last still blocked on the real forecaster), the paper's Table V fix, or the real LSTM forecaster/API facade.
**Hard Rules check:** Hard Rule 10 (by analogy, central this session): every tenant identity, sensitivity_class, pqc_capable flag, action, and served tier rendered is a real field off a real `LivingSystemSnapshot`, verified by the no-fabrication test and the exact-color per-tier tests, not just design intent; the real `env._resulting_key_type` ground-truth function is reused rather than re-derived, so colors cannot drift from what the environment actually did. Hard Rule 3: `env/environment.py`, `env/masking.py`, `env/request_generator.py` were read-only this session (confirmed via `git status --short`/`git diff --stat` showing only new files) -- the real tenant-graph-quirk finding above was investigated and flagged, not fixed, since fixing it would touch `env/forecast_provider.py` and needs sign-off first. Hard Rule 7: the mid-episode narrative was corrected against what the real data actually showed (the ratchet does not wait for S2's scripted schedule) rather than shipping a design based on an unverified assumption; the snapshot selection logic was rewritten around real milestones once this was found, not left inaccurate in a comment.

### [SOLO — render dose-response + S3 comparison demo visuals from real measured data] — 2026-08-29 — main

**Branch sync confirmed before starting:** `git rev-parse main dev21` both `9b891bbbc5a80f85686da3d3ef43c0d3b338517d`.

**Session goal:** the review/demo is the near-term priority. - **Calibrated wall-time before committing to a full re-run**: reloading one S3 checkpoint + an 8-eval-seed `evaluate_multi_seed` call took 0.91s (after a one-time ~5.8s import/construction cost); one alpha of `evaluate_attack_multi_seed` (5 eval seeds) on a reloaded S2 checkpoint took 0.63s. Full cost for everything this session needs (6 checkpoints x 8 eval seeds for S3; 2 checkpoints x 11 alphas x 5 eval seeds for S5): well under a minute.
- **Chose to re-run, not transcribe** -- per the session's own strong preference. Before writing the real driver, spot-checked reproducibility directly: reloaded `checkpoints/s3_masked_seed1.pt` and re-ran its real `evaluate_multi_seed` call -- result (`below_floor_rate=0.0000`, `total_reward=-8373.495...`, `forced_rekey_ratio=0.19640...`, `p99_latency=1.5`) matched SESSION_LOG.md's own per-seed table **exactly** (`-8373.50`/`0.196`). Reloaded `checkpoints/dqn_s2.pt` and re-ran alpha=0.9 of `evaluate_attack_multi_seed` -- result (`0.3000 +/- 0.0076`) matched the sweep table exactly. Confirmed these are the same trained weights the original sessions produced, not a drifted or different set, before building anything on top of them.
- The fresh re-run's full numeric output (both the S3 comparison and the S5 sweep) matched SESSION_LOG.md's prose numbers to the 4 decimal places already reported there, across every alpha/metric -- no discrepancy found. See `dashboard/samples/results_data.json` (this session's saved raw-results file, so a future session has a real file to prefer over re-running or re-transcribing) for the full-precision fresh numbers.

**What got done:**
- `dashboard/render_dose_response.py` (new): `DoseResponsePoint(alpha, mean, std)` / `DoseResponseSeries(label, series_key, points)` -- a thin, real-field-projecting input contract (populated 1:1 from `MultiSeedAttackEvalResult.alpha`/`.below_floor_rate_true_mean`/`_std`, never re-derived). `render_trace_html`-style pure function `render_dose_response_html(series, ...)` draws an inline SVG line chart (polyline + circles + vertical error-bar lines per series, y-axis gridlines auto-scaled off the real data's own max) styled to the mockup's dose-chart look (masked = `--hybrid` green, soft-reward = `--danger` red, matching the mockup's own legend color convention). **The one annotation this module adds (the boundary callout) is itself computed directly from the input series** (`_first_nonzero`: the first point, in ascending-alpha order, whose real mean is `> 0`) -- never a hardcoded alpha, so it can't misdescribe a different dataset than the one actually rendered; a series that's genuinely flat zero everywhere gets an honest "flat zero across the full range shown" callout instead of a fabricated boundary.
- `dashboard/render_comparison_table.py` (new): `AgentMetrics` (one agent's real, checkpoint-averaged S3 metrics) / `ComparisonTableData(scenario, masked, soft_reward, include_p99=True)`. `render_comparison_table_html(data, ...)` renders `below_floor_rate` as the leading (`hero`-styled) row, deliberately, per instruction; if `include_p99` is True the `p99_latency` row is always paired with a fixed caveat block quoting `experiments/harness.py::ScenarioResult.p99_latency`'s own documented mechanism (never rendered without it -- see the test below); `regret_events` is always shown with a `(== pool_exhaustion_events)` label plus a standing note that they're the same event by construction, quoting `run_scenario`'s own docstring.
- `dashboard/render_results_demo.py` (new): the real driver. `collect_real_s3_comparison()` reloads the 6 real S3 checkpoints (via a `DQNAgent`/`GreedyDQNPolicy` reconstruction mirroring `experiments/train.py::train()`'s own `state_dim`/`has_forecast`/`load_dqn_config()` derivation), re-runs `evaluate_multi_seed` (8 eval seeds, matching the original session) against each, and aggregates the 3 per-training-seed `MultiSeedEvalResult`s into one `AgentMetrics` via mean/std across those 3 already-eval-seed-averaged values -- the exact "checkpoint-averaged, training-seed std" methodology Gate W3 and the original S3 comparison session both used (verified to reproduce `-10214.82`/`1303.25` exactly). `collect_real_dose_response()` reloads the 2 real S2 checkpoints and re-runs `evaluate_attack_multi_seed` across the real 11-alpha grid (5 eval seeds each, matching the original sweep), projecting each `MultiSeedAttackEvalResult` into a `DoseResponsePoint`. `main()` re-runs both, saves the full real output to `dashboard/samples/results_data.json` (with an explicit `"provenance"` field describing exactly how it was produced), then renders both HTML files from the SAME live objects the JSON was built from (never a re-parse of the JSON) -- so the saved file and the rendered pages are guaranteed to describe one real run, not two. Run via `python -m dashboard.render_results_demo`.
- Ran the driver for real: **fresh output matched SESSION_LOG.md's prior numbers exactly** -- masked `below_floor_rate=0.0000`, soft-reward `0.1687`; masked dose-response has 6/11 alpha points with nonzero `V(pi)` (alpha 0.5-1.0), soft-reward has 11/11 (already nonzero at alpha=0.0, per the agent's structural unmanipulated-baseline property). Wrote `dashboard/samples/results_data.json`, `dashboard/samples/s3_comparison_table.html`, `dashboard/samples/dose_response_chart.html`.
- `tests/test_render_dose_response.py` (new, 9 tests): every real (alpha, mean, std) point (from the actual SESSION_LOG.md S5 table) appears verbatim in the chart's `data-*` attributes, in order, for one and for two series; **the central Hard Rule 7 tests** -- `test_masked_agent_alpha_0_9_nonzero_value_is_faithfully_shown` (asserts the exact real `alpha=0.9`/`mean=0.3`/`std=0.0076` triple appears) and `test_masked_curve_is_not_flat_zero_everywhere` (asserts `max(means) == 0.3`, guarding specifically against the tempting-but-false flat-zero-everywhere render); the boundary callout names the real first-nonzero alpha (0.5) and the real plateau value (0.3000) computed from the input, not hardcoded; a flat-zero synthetic series gets the honest "flat zero" callout, not a fabricated boundary; a no-fabrication check that every rendered `(alpha, mean, std)` triple is a member of the real input set; HTML well-formedness (balanced-tag `HTMLParser` subclass, same pattern as last session, no new dependency); a `write_dose_response_html` round-trip test.
- `tests/test_render_comparison_table.py` (new, 10 tests): `below_floor_rate` provably precedes `total_reward`/`forced_rekey_ratio` in the rendered row order; the real total_reward/forced_rekey_ratio/regret/floor_violations_total values appear; **the p99 honesty test** (`test_p99_latency_always_carries_its_caveat_when_shown`) asserts the caveat text is present whenever `include_p99=True`, and a companion test asserts `p99_latency`/its caveat/its value are ALL absent when `include_p99=False` (no orphaned caveat); the regret==pool_exhaustion annotation is always present; a no-fabrication check parsing every `<tbody>` row's two numeric leads and asserting each is exactly one of the real `AgentMetrics` fields at this renderer's own documented formatting precision; HTML well-formedness with and without the p99 row; a `write_comparison_table_html` round-trip test.
- Full `pytest` suite: **597 passed, 1 xfailed** (578 prior + 19 new), ~30s.

### [SOLO — render Explain Decision panel from real decision traces] — 2026-08-29 — main

**Session goal:** First VISUAL/frontend piece of the project (every prior session was backend/experiment work). `dashboard/explain.py`'s Explain Decision backend has been real+tested since 2026-08-19 (see that date's entry); no dashboard UI has ever rendered its output. Build a rendering layer that turns a real `DecisionTrace` into the mockup's visual layout, driven entirely by real computed values (Hard Rule 10) -- and only this one panel, not the other six.

**Read first, per instruction:** `PROGRESS.md` in full (confirmed `dashboard/explain.py` implemented+tested, `dashboard/app.py` not-started, `dashboard/mockups/smartkeynet_dashboard_mockup_v2.html` the only visual artifact, fabricated example data per its own header); the 2026-08-19 "`dashboard/explain.py`, Explain Decision backend" SESSION_LOG entry (the exact `DecisionTrace` shape/six-step contract); `dashboard/explain.py` in full (`DecisionTrace`/`MaskEntry`/`CostEntry` dataclasses, `explain_decision`/`explain_decision_from_env` signatures); the mockup's Explain Decision tab (HTML structure + CSS + its `renderTrace()` JS, for visual/layout reference only -- confirmed its `requests` array data is 100% hand-authored, never read); `env/contracts.py` (`Action`/`SensitivityClass`/`ThreatPosture` enums -- confirms the mockup's 4x3 floor grid shape is real, not mockup-specific) and `env/masking.py`'s `_PLACEHOLDER_FLOOR_TABLE`/`compute_mask` (read-only, ground truth); `experiments/harness.py::run_scenario`'s reset/step loop shape, mirrored by the driver below.

**Design decision (recorded here, per instruction):** a plain Python function (`dashboard/render_explain.py::render_trace_html`) taking a real `DecisionTrace` and returning a self-contained HTML string (inline CSS, zero JS, zero server, zero build step) styled to match the mockup's Explain Decision tab. Confirmed this adds **zero new dependencies** -- deliberately does not reach for `dash`/`plotly` (both already in `requirements.txt` for the *eventual* full live dashboard, `dashboard/app.py`, still a stub) since that would be disproportionate for rendering one already-computed trace object into one static page, per this session's explicit brief. Lives at `dashboard/render_explain.py` (matches `dashboard/`'s existing module-naming convention), imports and consumes `dashboard.explain`'s output only -- never reimplements any of its logic (verified: no import of `env.masking`/`env.environment` internals in the new file, only `dashboard.explain.DecisionTrace` and `env.contracts` enums for grid/label lookups).

**What got done:**
- `dashboard/render_explain.py` (new): `render_trace_html(trace, *, title=...)` -- pure function, six private `_render_stepN_*` helpers each reading only `DecisionTrace` fields (never computing a new value), string-templated into one HTML page with inline CSS adapted from the mockup's dark theme (`--bg`/`--panel`/`--quantum`/`--classical`/`--pqc`/`--hybrid` etc.), using system font stacks instead of the mockup's Google Fonts `<link>` (keeps the file genuinely zero-network-dependency, openable offline). `write_trace_html(trace, path, *, title=...)` writes it to disk, creating parent dirs. Every dynamic string is passed through `html.escape` before inlining. Floor-grid cells carry a `data-cell="{SensClass}-{Posture}"` attribute (mirrors the mockup's own JS hook) so the fired cell can be verified programmatically, not just visually.
- `dashboard/render_explain_demo.py` (new): the small, real driver. `collect_real_traces(seed=0, max_steps=250)` loads the real, committed `configs/scenarios/s2_hndl.yaml` via `experiments.train.load_full_config`, runs a full real S2 episode through `SmartKeyNetEnv` under `agents.baselines.StaticThresholdPolicy(0.5)` (an existing, real baseline -- not a hand-authored input), calling `explain_decision_from_env` once per decision (before each `env.step()`, per that function's documented contract, mirroring `experiments/harness.py::run_scenario`'s loop shape exactly). `pick_demo_traces` selects three genuinely different real decisions from that one real episode: the first decision, the first decision where `floor is Action.SERVE_HYBRID` (only one SERVE tier clears the floor -- the floor-driven case), and the first with a genuine multi-cost tradeoff (>=2 legal actions resolving to *different* costs, not a same-cost tie). `main()` writes all three to `dashboard/samples/*.html` -- these are the findable sample outputs for demo/review: `dashboard/samples/01_first_decision.html`, `02_floor_driven_only_hybrid_clears.html`, `03_real_cost_tradeoff.html`. Run via `python -m dashboard.render_explain_demo`.
- **Real investigation, not assumed**: the brief asked for a floor-driven case that "visibly shows no cost tradeoff existed" -- `dashboard/explain.py`'s own `cost_note` field is exactly that signal, but only fires when literally every non-tier action is illegal too. Traced this structurally through `env/masking.py::compute_mask`'s real rules: `REKEY_NOW`'s action index (4) always exceeds every real floor value (0-2), so it has no illegality rule at all and is essentially always legal once any tier clears the floor -- meaning a true single-legal-action mask requires the rare joint condition of pool exhaustion *and* an existing session's tier having fallen behind an already-risen floor, at the exact same decision. Searched for this directly and honestly, not by assumption: swept 100+ seeds across both real elevated-threat configs (`s2_hndl.yaml`, `s3_degradation.yaml`) under all four real baseline policies in `agents/baselines.py` (`AlwaysHybridPolicy`, `AlwaysPQCPolicy`, `StaticThresholdPolicy` at two thresholds, `RandomPolicy`), including deliberately-scaled-down pool configs to force exhaustion -- **never found it occurring from a real, unmanipulated episode.** Root cause, verified: the only way to actually exhaust the pool is aggressive `SERVE_HYBRID` consumption, but doing so is exactly what keeps every serviced tenant's session tier at/above `HYBRID` (so `REUSE` stays legal for them going forward) -- a structural tension between the two conditions needed. The `cost_note`/single-legal-action branch is real and directly tested (`tests/test_explain.py::test_only_one_legal_action_gets_a_cost_note`, a constructed-input test, same edge case this session's `tests/test_render_explain.py` also exercises), but this is a genuine finding that it is a rare synthetic edge case under current calibration, not something a real baseline-policy episode naturally reaches -- documented in `dashboard/render_explain_demo.py`'s own module docstring rather than silently worked around. The driver's floor-driven selector therefore uses "only one SERVE tier clears the floor" (`floor is Action.SERVE_HYBRID`, real and common) instead, and lets that decision's own real `cost_note`/`costs` display honestly (in the actual sample, a real same-cost `SERVE_HYBRID`/`REKEY_NOW` tie -- itself a faithful view of the real mask, not fabricated).
- `tests/test_render_explain.py` (new, 10 real behavioral tests, no smoke tests): every trace rendered in these tests comes from calling the real `explain_decision`/`explain_decision_from_env` functions (constructed-but-real inputs for targeted cases, a real stepped `SmartKeyNetEnv` for the end-to-end cases) -- never a hand-built `DecisionTrace`. Covers: the real floor/chosen-action/final-sentence appearing verbatim (HTML-escaped) in the output; every real per-action mask reason appearing; real cost numbers for a genuine tradeoff case (`test_render_shows_real_cost_numbers_for_a_genuine_tradeoff`, matching this session's own driver-selection logic); the real `cost_note` "only one legal action" case rendering correctly (`test_render_shows_the_real_cost_note_when_only_one_legal_action_exists`, the synthetic edge case from the investigation above, built via `explain_decision` directly with the same inputs `test_explain.py`'s own equivalent test uses); **the floor-grid `hit` cell test** (`test_floor_grid_highlights_exactly_the_traces_real_floor_cell`) -- parametrized across all 12 `(SensitivityClass, ThreatPosture)` cells via a fresh `PolicyTable()` per case, asserting the rendered `data-cell` carrying the `hit` class is exactly the trace's own `floor_cell`, never a hardcoded one; **the Hard Rule 10 tests** (`test_hard_rule_10_every_rendered_reason_and_final_text_comes_from_the_trace`, `test_hard_rule_10_cost_rows_are_exactly_the_traces_own_legal_actions`) -- step a real `SmartKeyNetEnv` for 15 decisions, and for each one assert the rendered per-action reasons list, final-sentence text, and cost-row action-name ordering are *exactly* (`==`, not just `in`) the trace's own real fields, HTML-escaped, never a different or invented string; two HTML well-formedness tests using a small balanced-tag `HTMLParser` subclass (no new dependency -- `bs4`/`lxml` aren't in `requirements.txt`); a `write_trace_html` round-trip test. Full suite: **578 passed, 1 xfailed** (568 prior + 10 new), ~34s.

**What's working:** The zero-drift design holds -- `render_trace_html` never recomputes anything `dashboard/explain.py` already computed, verified directly by the Hard Rule 10 exact-match tests, not just by design intent. The driver's three real sample files, generated from one real S2 episode under a real baseline policy, visually match the mockup's Explain Decision tab layout while every number/reason/sentence in them is genuinely computed.
**What's broken / incomplete:** Only the Explain Decision panel is rendered -- the other six dashboard panels (Living System, Budgeting Brain, Steering Attack, Migration Wave, Results, Threat Input) remain mockup-only, deliberately out of scope this session. The real "no cost tradeoff, only one legal action" `cost_note` branch, while real and tested, could not be demonstrated from an actual unmanipulated episode run (see investigation above) -- the driver's floor-driven sample instead shows the more common "only one tier clears, but REKEY_NOW ties it" real shape.
**Blockers:** None for this panel. The Threat Input panel (§7.1) remains blocked on Person A's feature-extraction work, unchanged from 2026-08-19's entry.
**Next session will:** Pick up one of: the remaining six dashboard panels (each needs its own backend+render work, per PROGRESS.md's "Next task"), the real LSTM forecaster (Addition A), or the API facade -- none started this session by design (this session deliberately did ONE panel, the one whose backend already existed).
**Hard Rules check:** Hard Rule 10 (central): verified directly, not just by design intent -- `tests/test_render_explain.py`'s two Hard Rule 10 tests assert exact (not substring) equality between rendered text and the real trace's own fields across 15 real decisions plus targeted constructed cases; the renderer's own module docstring states the constraint and points at these tests. Hard Rules 1/2/3: not touched -- `env/contracts.py`, `dashboard/explain.py`, `env/environment.py`, `env/masking.py`, `agents/*`, `experiments/*`, `reward.*` in any config, and the mockup HTML file are all read-only this session, confirmed via `git status --short`/`git diff --stat` showing only new files (`dashboard/render_explain.py`, `dashboard/render_explain_demo.py`, `dashboard/samples/*.html`, `tests/test_render_explain.py`) -- zero modified files. No point this session tempted a change to any of those; the pool-exhaustion investigation used config *overrides* passed at construction time (`load_full_config(...)` then mutating the returned dict in a scratch script), never edits to the committed scenario YAML files themselves.

### [SOLO — root-cause soft-reward baseline's S5 dose-response curve shape, diagnostic] — 2026-08-27 — main

**Branch sync check, before starting — found real divergence, stopped and reported per instruction:** `git rev-parse main dev21` returned DIFFERENT hashes (`main`=`81a1a17` "Create steering_attack_limitations_addendum.md"; `dev21`=`4669278`, the prior S5-sweep session's own commit). Merge-base = `dev21`'s tip, i.e. a clean fast-forward (`main` exactly one commit ahead), not a true conflicting divergence. Reported exactly this to the user before proceeding; directed to fast-forward `dev21` to `main` immediately (`git branch -f dev21 main`). **Confirmed identical afterward: both `main` and `dev21` = `81a1a174a6ea8e42166c370f3951e6fb2c9f8f71`.**

**Housekeeping note (unrelated to this session's own investigation, flagged so it isn't lost before final paper drafting):** a paper-limitations addendum file, added to the repo outside this session's own work, exists at **`docs/steering_attack_limitations_addendum.md`** (81 lines, commit `81a1a17`, 2026-08-27). Its contents were deliberately NOT read into or used by this session's investigation, per instruction — noted here, and in PROGRESS.md, purely as a pointer so whoever does final paper integration knows it exists and where.

**Session goal:** PROGRESS.md's one open item from the 2026-08-26 S5 sweep session — the soft-reward baseline agent's own `V(pi)` dose-response curve (flat at `0.2896` through alpha=0.8, step to `0.3256` at alpha>=0.9, off an already-substantial alpha=0 baseline) was reported honestly but never mechanistically investigated, unlike the masked agent's own high-alpha finding (which got a full per-decision trace). This session's entire job was that investigation, to the same rigor.

**Read first:** PROGRESS.md in full; SESSION_LOG.md's last 4 entries in full (S5 sweep, attack generator, masked-vs-soft-reward S3 comparison — the `0.1687` unmanipulated-baseline-violation number, soft-reward agent build); `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py` in full; `env/contracts.py`'s `StateDict`; `attack/attacking_provider.py`; `env/masking.py`'s `PolicyTable`/one-way ratchet; `env/environment.py`'s `_prepare_decision` (the `argmax(posture_probs)` -> `ratchet_up` -> `policy_floor` wiring). No raw per-decision sweep data was saved from the prior session (its own scripts were scratchpad-only, per that session's own log) — the S2 checkpoints themselves (`checkpoints/{dqn,soft_reward_baseline}_s2.pt`) DID survive on disk (gitignored, not deleted), so this session reused them directly rather than retraining.

**Finding #1 — direct code-level answer, the single most important thing established first:** `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py::compute_soft_reward` (lines 184-211) reads exactly two `state` fields: `state["policy_floor"]` and `state["key_type_onehot"]` — it does **NOT** read `threat_score`, `threat_forecast`, or `posture_probs` anywhere, confirmed by direct inspection, not a grep proxy. `security_score(tier)` (line 177) is a pure 3-value lookup (`classical=0.2, pqc=0.6, hybrid=1.0`) keyed **only** on `delivered_tier` — for `SERVE_CLASSICAL`/`SERVE_PQC`/`SERVE_HYBRID`/an established `REUSE`, `delivered_tier` returns the action/existing-tier unchanged, with **zero** dependence on `floor` (`delivered_tier`, line 153: `if action in _TIER_ACTIONS: return action`; the `REUSE` branch returns `existing`, ignoring the `floor` argument entirely). So for the large majority of decision types, this agent's reward has **no posture dependence of any kind, direct or indirect, in the reward computation itself** — confirming the working hypothesis from PROGRESS.md's "Next task."

**Finding #1a — the one real, narrow exception, found by reading `delivered_tier`/`resolved_cost_action` completely, not just `compute_soft_reward`'s own top level:** for `REKEY_NOW` (and cold-start `REUSE`/`REKEY_NOW`, no session key yet established), `delivered_tier` resolves to `max(existing tier, floor)` (or `floor` alone on cold start) — `floor` here IS `state["policy_floor"]`, which `env/environment.py::_prepare_decision` computes every step as `self._policy_table.floor(sensitivity_class, argmax(threat_forecast.posture_probs))` after `ratchet_up`, using the **live forecaster's own posture reading** — the attacked one, under `AttackingForecastProvider`, exactly the same one-way-ratchet `PolicyTable` mechanism the masked agent's own floor uses (verified in `env/environment.py`, `security_masking` only changes what `compute_mask()` is given, never what `state["policy_floor"]`/`self._current_floor` themselves carry — design decision 16's own docstring already says this, re-confirmed here by reading the actual `_prepare_decision` code path). **So `compute_soft_reward` DOES have a real, direct (not merely Q-network-observation-mediated) posture dependence, but only for `REKEY_NOW`/cold-start decisions, via the discrete, ratchet-processed `policy_floor` — never via a continuous `threat_score`/`posture_probs` term, and never for a direct tier action or an already-established `REUSE`.**

**Paper-alignment flag, per Hard Rule 7 — reported plainly, not softened:** if the paper draft's Fig. 5 narrative describes `p̂_t` (a continuous estimated-threat/posture quantity) as entering this agent's reward with "the security term shrinking" as posture is attacked, **that does not match what was actually built.** What was actually built: a reward with a *fixed*, tier-only security term (Hard Rule-1-tension note in the module docstring: reproduces Noetzold's own simpler formula, deliberately no continuous threat-weighting), plus one narrow, discrete, ratchet-gated side-channel (`policy_floor` feeding `REKEY_NOW`'s resolution only). This is a real, useful, and honestly-described design (a genuine reproduction of the reference formula), but it is not "posture enters the reward and shrinks the security term" — it is "posture, once ratcheted to a discrete floor, determines what a small subset of actions deliver." **Flagged here as a concrete item for whoever does final paper integration**, not silently reshaped to match the existing draft language.

**Finding #2 — the discrete-threshold hypothesis, tested directly with real numbers, same style as the masked agent's own high-alpha investigation:** reused the real, still-on-disk `checkpoints/soft_reward_baseline_s2.pt` (no retraining needed) and replayed the same 5 eval seeds (`900-904`) at a finer alpha grid (`0.0, 0.5, 0.7, 0.75, 0.8, 0.85, 0.88, 0.9, 0.92, 0.95, 1.0`) than the original 11-point sweep, this time also capturing the ATTACKED posture bucket (`argmax(attacking_provider.get_threat_forecast().posture_probs)`) alongside the TRUE one at every decision (scratchpad script, not committed):

| alpha | `V(π)_true` (mean, 5 seeds) | bucket-mismatch rate (attacked ≠ true posture bucket) |
|---|---|---|
| 0.00 | 0.2896 | 0.0000 |
| 0.50 | 0.2896 | 0.0032 |
| 0.70 | 0.2896 | 0.0080 |
| 0.75 | 0.2896 | 0.0112 |
| 0.80 | 0.2896 | 0.0224 |
| **0.85** | **0.3256** | **0.8112** |
| 0.88-1.00 | 0.3256 | 0.8112 |

The step in `V(π)_true` (`0.2896 -> 0.3256`) and the jump in posture-bucket mismatch rate (`0.0224 -> 0.8112`) land in **exactly the same alpha interval** (`0.80 -> 0.85`), pinpointing the crossing more precisely than the original 11-point grid's `0.8 -> 0.9` resolution could. Every single mismatch across the whole grid is an **understatement** (`bucket_mismatch_rate == bucket_understate_rate` exactly, at every alpha) — the attack, by construction (equation 7 pulling toward `g(x)=[0,0]`, the benign-region floor), never causes the attacked posture to read HIGHER than the true one. **This is the same HIGH->ELEVATED one-way-ratchet-relevant discrete crossing mechanism already established for the masked agent's own alpha>=0.9 finding — real, precise, corroborating evidence, not an assumed parallel.**

**Finding #3 — the controlled same-state-except-attacked-posture comparison, run properly (not on a single anecdote):** the first attempt (one hand-picked `REUSE` decision at alpha=0.9) showed no action change, but `REUSE`'s `delivered_tier` ignores `floor` entirely (Finding #1) — the wrong decision type to test the hypothesis on. Re-ran across **every** tier-establishing decision (`SERVE_*`/`REKEY_NOW`, where `floor` genuinely feeds the resolution) at alpha=0.9, all 5 eval seeds: **100 tier-establishing decisions total, 16 with a true/attacked posture-bucket divergence.** For each of those 16, built a true-posture-substituted `StateDict` (only `threat_score`/`threat_forecast`/`policy_floor` swapped for the shadow-tracked true values, everything else byte-identical — `qber`/`skr`/`pool_fill`/`load`/`key_age`/`key_type_onehot`/`sensitivity_class`/foresight fields/`regret_event_recent` all held fixed) and re-ran the frozen `GreedyDQNPolicy`:

- **The Q-network's argmax ACTION changed in 0/16 cases** — at every one of these 16 decisions the agent picks `REKEY_NOW` regardless of whether it's shown the attacked or the true posture/floor. The prior session's own hypothesis ("the small shift... comes from its frozen Q-network seeing different threat_score/policy_floor inputs... choosing differently") is **not supported by this direct test** — the action choice itself is not what changes.
- **But the DELIVERED TIER changed in 6/16 of those same cases** (`900`@66,74,95; `902`@74,126; `903`@71 — e.g. `900`@66: attacked floor=1 -> delivers `SERVE_PQC`; true floor=2 -> would have delivered `SERVE_HYBRID`), purely because `REKEY_NOW`'s resolution formula (`max(existing, floor)`) reads `state["policy_floor"]` directly (Finding #1a) — the SAME chosen action, `REKEY_NOW`, delivers a materially lower tier once the attack has pulled `policy_floor` down. **This is the real, precise mechanism, more specific than the prior session's Q-network-observation hypothesis: it is not that attacked posture changes what the agent decides to do; it is that the environment's own deterministic resolution of what a fixed decision (`REKEY_NOW`) delivers depends directly on the attacked floor.**

**Finding #4 — the already-substantial alpha=0 baseline is confirmed structural, not attack-related, exactly as PROGRESS.md's open item speculated:** at alpha=0.0 the bucket-mismatch rate is exactly `0.0000` (no attack possible, attacked==true trivially) yet `V(π)_true` is already `0.2896`. This matches the already-established, unrelated S3 finding (masked-vs-soft-reward S3 comparison session: unmanipulated below-floor rate `0.1687`) — this agent's reward has no incentive to proactively rekey once it holds a tier (`security_score` only rewards whatever tier is currently delivered, and `REUSE` is cheap), so once S2's own scripted `elevate_at_step=50` ratchets the TRUE floor up mid-episode, an already-established lower-tier session key just keeps getting `REUSE`d indefinitely — a property of this agent's reward design, present with or without any attacker.

**Verdict, per Hard Rule 7 — resolved, not partially open:** the soft-reward baseline's S5 curve shape is now understood to the same rigor as the masked agent's own high-alpha finding, with real, direct evidence at every step (not an inferred narrative):
1. The reward has (almost) no posture dependence — confirmed from the actual code, not assumed — except one narrow, discrete channel (`policy_floor` -> `REKEY_NOW`'s resolution only).
2. The flat-then-step shape is explained by the same discrete-posture-bucket-crossing mechanism as the masked agent's own curve, with the exact alpha interval (`0.80->0.85`) numerically pinned down via a real, finer-resolution re-run of the existing checkpoints (no retraining needed).
3. The mechanism is a delivered-tier effect (`REKEY_NOW`'s resolution formula reading the attacked `policy_floor`), NOT a Q-network action-choice effect (0/16 real, tested decisions changed action; 6/16 changed delivered tier at the same, unchanged action).
4. The already-substantial alpha=0 baseline is confirmed structural (this agent's known no-rekey-incentive property, same root cause as the already-documented S3 `0.1687` finding), not an attack artifact.

**What got done:** zero changes to any tracked file (`git status --porcelain` / `git diff --stat` both empty throughout). Two scratchpad-only diagnostic scripts (not committed, same status as prior sessions' own scratchpad sweep scripts): a finer-grained per-alpha discrete-threshold probe, and a full controlled same-state-except-posture comparison across every tier-establishing decision at alpha=0.9. Both reused the real, already-trained, still-on-disk `checkpoints/soft_reward_baseline_s2.pt` (and `dqn_s2.pt`, unused this session) — no retraining.

**Full test results: 568 passed, 1 xfailed — unchanged, zero regressions** (identical to the prior session's own count; this session made no code changes for a regression to even be possible).

**What's working:** the soft-reward baseline's S5 curve shape is now a fully resolved, evidence-backed mechanism, not an open item.
**What's broken / incomplete:** none for this session's own scope. The paper draft's existing "p̂_t enters the reward" language (if present) needs revision to match what was actually built — flagged above and in PROGRESS.md as a concrete item for final paper integration, not fixed here (out of scope; no paper file exists in this repo to edit).
**Blockers:** none.
**Next session will:** per updated PROGRESS.md — whoever does final paper drafting should read this entry's "Paper-alignment flag" and PROGRESS.md's restated pointer to `docs/steering_attack_limitations_addendum.md` before writing the Limitations/Fig. 5 sections. Otherwise, the standing lower-priority items (posture-ceiling fix, real LSTM forecaster, Dashboard v2) are unchanged.
**Hard Rules check:** Hard Rule 7 — the central discipline all session: the controlled comparison's real result (0/16 action changes) contradicted the prior session's own softer hypothesis, and is reported as a correction rather than smoothed to agree with it; the paper-alignment mismatch is flagged plainly, not reshaped to fit existing draft language. Hard Rule 1 — `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py`'s reward formula was read and analyzed in exhaustive detail but never modified (`git diff --stat` empty); `agents/dqn.py` likewise untouched. Hard Rule 2 — re-confirmed, not re-tested: `policy_floor`'s one-way ratchet is exactly the same mechanism already verified for the masked agent; this session traced how the SOFT-REWARD agent's reward reads that same value, not a new masking property. `env/contracts.py`, `configs/soft_reward_baseline*.yaml`: confirmed untouched throughout.

### [SOLO — S5 steering-attack dose-response sweep: masked DQN vs soft-reward baseline] — 2026-08-26 — main

**Branch sync confirmed before starting:** `git rev-parse main dev21` both `e4e6d6f3a849ac640d0c9809ff3149fef90c2b33`.

**Session goal, two parts:** Part 1 — build a live `ForecastProvider` that applies the already-real, already-tested `attack/trace_generator.py::generate_adversarial_window` equation-7 attack to a running episode, plus dual-tracking measurement code that reports the real, TRUE-posture-referenced below-floor service rate (paper draft equation 4, `V(pi)`) alongside what the agent actually saw under attack. Part 2 — only after Part 1's own tests pass — run the real 11-point alpha sweep on S2 (masked DQN vs. the soft-reward baseline) and report `V(pi)` honestly, including if it doesn't match the pre-registered "masked stays flat at zero everywhere" prediction.

**Read first:** PROGRESS.md's "Next task" and the standing posture-ceiling item; SESSION_LOG.md's trace-generator/p99-latency/masked-vs-soft-reward S3 entries; PLAN.md's Hard Rules 1/2/3/7, Analogy 2, and the S5 table row; `attack/trace_generator.py` in full (real, tested, untouched this session); `env/forecast_provider.py`'s `MovingAverageForecaster`/`ForecastProvider` interface; `env/environment.py`'s `__init__`/`reset()`/`_advance_to_next_decision`/`_prepare_decision` in full; `experiments/harness.py`'s `run_scenario`/`evaluate_multi_seed` in full; `configs/scenarios/s2_hndl.yaml`.

**Part 1, finding #1 (flagged, signed off before building):** step 6's own working assumption — "confirm `env/environment.py` already has a clean forecaster injection point, same pattern as `request_stream_factory`" — turned out FALSE on inspection. `__init__` only ever took `config`/`request_stream_factory`; `reset()`'s `self._forecaster = self._build_forecaster()` is the only forecaster-construction site, hardcoded to read `config["use_foresight"]` directly, with no swap point at all. Flagged via `AskUserQuestion` per this file's own "stop and flag before touching a protected file" convention; signed off to add a **second, narrowly-scoped instance of the identical `request_stream_factory` pattern** (design decision 12): `forecast_provider_factory: Callable[[int | None], ForecastProvider | None] | None = None`, same shape, same default-`None`-preserves-prior-behavior guarantee, used in `reset()` as `self._forecaster = self._forecast_provider_factory(episode_seed) if ... else self._build_forecaster()`. Module docstring gained design decision 17, mirroring 12's own text almost verbatim.

**Backward-compatibility proof, same rigor as the 2026-08-24 `experiments/train.py` scenario-parameter session — a real stashed-diff before/after comparison, not just "tests pass":** a scratchpad script ran a full `RandomPolicy`-driven episode (no `request_stream_factory`, no `forecast_provider_factory` — the exact default path every pre-existing caller uses) on both S1-default and S2-ewma configs, recording the full per-decision action/reward/`policy_floor`/`key_type_onehot` trace plus the final forecaster's exact `get_threat_forecast()` output. Ran once against this session's changed `env/environment.py`, `git stash`'d the diff, ran again against the real, unmodified pre-change code, `git stash pop`'d. **Byte-for-byte identical** on both configs (full traces, final `total_reward`, final forecaster state) — confirmed programmatically (`a == b` on the full JSON), not eyeballed.

**Part 1, `attack/attacking_provider.py::AttackingForecastProvider` (new file):** wraps a `base_provider`; `update()` shapes only `ForecastObservation["threat_features"]` via the existing, untouched `generate_adversarial_window` before forwarding to `base_provider` (every other field — qber/skr/pool_fill/arrivals/hybrid_serves — passes through unchanged, since equation 7 and PLAN.md Addition A's pool-head/threat-head separation both scope the attack to the threat channel only). An optional `shadow_provider` receives the TRUE, unshaped observation on every `update()` call, for measurement only — this is the only place in the codebase ever handed both the true window and a place to fork it, since `environment.py` never exposes a raw `ForecastObservation` outside its own tick loop. `get_true_threat_forecast()` exposes the shadow's output; raises if no shadow was given, rather than silently returning the attacked forecast.

**Part 1 tests (`tests/test_attacking_provider.py`, 6 tests) — proven before any sweep, per instruction:**
- alpha=0.0 exact equality with the unwrapped base, across a real varying multi-step sequence, both threat and pool heads, plus the fresh-instance (never-updated) case.
- alpha=1.0 exact equality with feeding `g(window)` directly into a plain provider.
- **The single most important test this session produces:** a constructed synthetic case (`true_window=[5.0,5.0]`, `alpha=1.0`, reusing the trace-generator session's own already-verified severe-window example) where the expected TRUE and ATTACKED steady-state `ThreatForecast`s are computed independently — two fresh, bare `MovingAverageForecaster` instances, fed directly, no dependency on `AttackingForecastProvider` at all — then `AttackingForecastProvider`'s own `get_threat_forecast()`/`get_true_threat_forecast()` are asserted exactly equal to those independently-derived values (`threat_score=0.9933071490757153` true / `0.5` exactly attacked; posture argmax HIGH true / ELEVATED attacked — a genuine discrete crossing, not an approximation).
- `get_true_threat_forecast()` raises without a `shadow_provider`.

**Part 1, dual-tracking measurement (`experiments/harness.py`, additive — `run_scenario_under_attack`/`AttackScenarioResult`/`evaluate_attack_multi_seed`/`MultiSeedAttackEvalResult`):** mirrors `run_scenario`/`evaluate_multi_seed`'s own shape and conventions. Per decision, a parallel, measurement-only shadow `PolicyTable` (fed the TRUE posture via `AttackingForecastProvider.get_true_threat_forecast()`, ratcheted the same sticky, one-way way `env/masking.py::PolicyTable` itself works) computes the TRUE floor for that exact request; the real, actually-delivered tier (`_delivered_tier`, the same helper `run_scenario` already uses) is compared against it. `below_floor_rate_true` is equation 4's real `V(pi)` — genuinely different from the pre-existing `ScenarioResult.floor_violations`/`below_floor_rate_*`, which compare delivered tier against the floor the agent **itself** saw (trivially 0 for any masked policy by construction — that's Hard Rule 2's narrow, literal guarantee, not equation 4's stronger claim). `true_floor_log`/`delivered_tier_log` are kept per-decision (also the raw material for a served-tier histogram, used in Part 2 below).

**Part 1 tests (`tests/test_harness.py`, +5; `tests/test_environment.py`, +3):**
- alpha=0.0 reduces `run_scenario_under_attack` to byte-for-byte the same episode/metrics as `run_scenario` (no attack, nothing for the TRUE track to disagree with).
- A masked policy's `V(pi)` is exactly `0.0` under full attack (Hard Rule 2's expected structural case, sanity-checked before the real sweep).
- **The harness-level divergence test, using real masking/floor computation, not a hardcoded expected rate:** `_AlwaysServeAttackedFloorPolicy` (serves exactly the tier the ATTACKED floor currently requires — by construction, zero violations against what it was shown) on real S2 under `security_masking: false`, full attack. Result: `scenario_result.floor_violations == 0` (never at fault relative to what it was told) yet `below_floor_true_count > 0`, independently recomputed from `true_floor_log`/`delivered_tier_log` (not just trusting the function's own counter) and matching exactly; a concrete instance found in the log where `true_floor == SERVE_HYBRID` and `delivered == SERVE_PQC`.
- `evaluate_attack_multi_seed` matches a manual list-comprehension re-run of `run_scenario_under_attack` per seed, and rejects empty `eval_seeds`.
- `env/environment.py`: `forecast_provider_factory=None` byte-for-byte identical to omitting it; a genuine drop-in swap (a trivial always-HIGH stub `ForecastProvider`, not `MovingAverageForecaster`) actually changes the live floor from the very first decision; the factory receives the same `episode_seed` `reset()` itself resolves.

**Full test results after Part 1: 568 passed, 1 xfailed** (554 prior + 14 new — 6 `test_attacking_provider.py`, 5 `test_harness.py`, 3 `test_environment.py`).

**Part 2 — training.** No S2 checkpoint existed for either agent (`ls checkpoints/` — only S1/S3-era files). New `configs/soft_reward_baseline_s2.yaml` (standalone, same one-file-per-scenario convention as the S3 variant — `pool:`/`security_masking: false`/`soft_reward:` copied verbatim from `configs/soft_reward_baseline.yaml`, `threat_schedule:` copied verbatim from `configs/scenarios/s2_hndl.yaml`, nothing re-tuned). Calibrated wall-time first (2,000-step timed runs: masked ~8.0s, soft-reward ~5.6s → ~100s/~70s extrapolated for the real 25,000-step campaigns) — S2 doesn't stress the pool at all, so, unlike the S3 comparison session, no multi-training-seed Gate-W3-level campaign was needed for this measurement session; one real 25,000-step run each (`train(scenario="S2")` / `train_soft_reward_baseline(scenario="S2")`, both seed 0, both configs' own defaults) sufficed and both converged (masked `reward_window_avgs` climbs from -139.6 to -17.5; soft-reward from -0.40 to 0.46) — `checkpoints/dqn_s2.pt`, `checkpoints/soft_reward_baseline_s2.pt` (both gitignored).

**Part 2 — sweep design.** 11 evenly-spaced alphas (`0.0, 0.1, ..., 1.0`, matching "the paper's eleven steps"). Calibrated wall-time first (2 eval seeds, 1 alpha, 1 agent: 0.28s) — negligible cost, so **5 eval seeds** (`900-904`) were used rather than the minimum 3, for a better spread estimate at essentially zero extra wall time (full 11×2×5 sweep: 13.8s total). Both agents evaluated frozen (`GreedyDQNPolicy`) via `evaluate_attack_multi_seed`, each in its own real, appropriate environment config (masked DQN under the real masked S2 config; soft-reward under its own `security_masking: false` S2 config) — matching the S3 comparison session's own precedent of evaluating each agent in its natural environment, not a shared one.

**Part 2 — the central finding, investigated in full per the pre-registered Hard Rule (a nonzero masked-agent `V(pi)` was to be treated as evidence of a masking bug, not quietly accepted):**

| alpha | masked DQN `V(pi)` (mean +/- std, 5 seeds) | soft-reward `V(pi)` (mean +/- std) |
|---|---|---|
| 0.0 | 0.0000 +/- 0.0000 | 0.2896 +/- 0.0678 |
| 0.1-0.4 | 0.0000 +/- 0.0000 | 0.2896 +/- 0.0678 |
| 0.5 | 0.0016 +/- 0.0020 | 0.2896 +/- 0.0678 |
| 0.6 | 0.0024 +/- 0.0020 | 0.2896 +/- 0.0678 |
| 0.7 | 0.0048 +/- 0.0039 | 0.2896 +/- 0.0678 |
| 0.8 | 0.0096 +/- 0.0032 | 0.2896 +/- 0.0678 |
| 0.9 | **0.3000 +/- 0.0076** | 0.3256 +/- 0.0799 |
| 1.0 | **0.3000 +/- 0.0076** | 0.3256 +/- 0.0799 |

The masked agent's `V(pi)` is **not** zero everywhere — it rises to `0.30` at alpha>=0.9. Investigated immediately, same rigor as Part 1's own tests, before writing up anything as valid:

1. **Ruled out a `compute_mask`/`PolicyTable` bug directly**: `scenario_result.floor_violations` (the masked agent's delivered tier vs. the floor it was actually shown — Hard Rule 2's literal, narrow guarantee) is exactly `0` at **every** seed, **every** alpha, `0.0` through `1.0`, no exception. The mask enforces the floor it computes perfectly; the code is not the problem.
2. **Traced the exact per-decision mechanism** (single-seed replay, seed 900): at alpha=0.7, exactly 2 violating decisions, both at ticks 47-48 (a brief transient lag right as S2's real `elevate_at_step=50` signal begins) — the ratchet catches the diluted-but-still-nonzero true signal within 2 decisions and correctly locks HIGH for the rest of the episode. At alpha=0.9/1.0, violations persist all the way to decision 236 of 250 — the ratchet **never fires** at all.
3. **Root cause**: `AttackingForecastProvider` (correctly, per its own tested contract) shapes *every* window from tick 1 onward, including the pre-`elevate_at_step` portion. At sufficiently high alpha, the live episode's own forecaster never receives enough real signal, at any point in the whole episode, to cross the HIGH-posture threshold — there is no "already-detected, then suppressed" floor for the one-way ratchet to defend, because the ratchet's sole channel of truth (the same forecaster the attack shapes) never detected anything in the first place. This is **not** a masking-code defect: Hard Rule 2's literal text ("threat signals may only raise floors") is satisfied exactly — the ratchet only ever moves up, and never in response to a floor it wasn't shown. It is a genuine, now precisely quantified **boundary of what the one-way ratchet can defend against**: it prevents an adversary from talking an *already-ratcheted* floor back down; it cannot manufacture a detection the sensor itself was never allowed to make. PLAN.md's own Limitations framing already anticipates exactly this class of gap in principle — this session supplies the first real, measured confirmation of it, not a new problem.

**Supplementary sweep (secondary, scratchpad only, not committed — isolates whether shaping the pre-elevation window specifically is what drives the gap):** a `_DelayedAttackingForecastProvider` that only shapes windows once internal tick count exceeds `delay_ticks=50` (S2's own `elevate_at_step`) — ticks 1-49 pass through completely unshaped to both the live and shadow tracks. Same checkpoints, same eval seeds, same alpha grid. **Result: nearly identical to the primary sweep** (masked `V(pi)`: `0.0000` through alpha 0.4, `0.0008/0.0016/0.0032/0.0096` at 0.5-0.8, `0.3000/0.3000` at 0.9/1.0 — same saturation point, same magnitude). This rules out "shaping the irrelevant pre-elevation noise" as the driver: the gap is entirely about continuous suppression **during and after** the real elevation window, exactly as the primary sweep's own per-decision trace (finding #2 above) already showed directly. This secondary analysis is confirmatory only — it does not soften or override the primary sweep's numbers above, which stand as measured.

**Soft-reward agent's own `V(pi)`, reported honestly per Hard Rule 7 (does not match the paper draft's clean "rises with alpha" prediction either):** already substantially nonzero at alpha=0.0 (`0.2896`, no attack at all) — expected, matching the prior session's unmanipulated S3 finding (this agent has no masking whatsoever, so it violates real floors as a baseline property of having no ratchet, independent of any attack). It does rise with alpha, but not smoothly — flat at `0.2896` through alpha=0.8, then a step up to `0.3256` at alpha>=0.9, the same "needs enough dilution to cross a threshold" shape the masked agent's own curve shows, just starting from a much higher floor. The predicted clean monotonic climb did not materialize; what's real is a modest (0.2896 -> 0.3256, ~12% relative) further degradation once the attack is strong enough to suppress essentially the entire real signal, on top of an already-substantial unattacked baseline violation rate.

**Served-tier histograms (from `delivered_tier_log`, summed across all 5 eval seeds' episodes, `SERVE_CLASSICAL`/`SERVE_PQC`/`SERVE_HYBRID` counts):** masked DQN shifts from `{CLASSICAL: 59, PQC: 172, HYBRID: 1019}` (alpha 0.0-0.6) to `{CLASSICAL: 315, PQC: 331, HYBRID: 604}` (alpha 0.9-1.0) — deprived of real threat signal, the agent correctly (from its own, fooled perspective) shifts toward cheaper tiers, since the floor it's shown rarely demands HYBRID. Soft-reward's own histogram barely moves (`{PQC: 462, HYBRID: 595, CLASSICAL: 193}` -> `{PQC: 464, HYBRID: 563, CLASSICAL: 223}`) — a much smaller effect, consistent with this agent's `compute_soft_reward` depending only on the chosen tier, not on posture; the small shift that does exist comes from its frozen Q-network seeing different `threat_score`/`policy_floor` state-vector inputs at eval time, not from its reward function reacting to the attack directly.

**Which alpha range was genuinely informative:** the full `0.0`-`1.0` range, unlike the earlier S3 comparison sessions — S2's own scripted `elevated_signal=6.0` deliberately overrides the standing posture-ceiling limitation (`env/forecast_provider.py`'s placeholder formula structurally cannot reach HIGH on ordinary in-domain `[qber, load]` values alone), so the TRUE track genuinely reaches HIGH and stays there for the back half of every episode, giving a real true-vs-attacked divergence to measure across the whole grid, not just a narrow sub-range.

**What got done:**
- `env/environment.py`: `forecast_provider_factory` constructor parameter (design decision 17), flagged and signed off before being built; module docstring updated; zero other behavior changed (proven via stashed-diff byte-for-byte comparison).
- `attack/attacking_provider.py` (new): `AttackingForecastProvider`.
- `experiments/harness.py`: `AttackScenarioResult`/`run_scenario_under_attack`/`MultiSeedAttackEvalResult`/`evaluate_attack_multi_seed` (additive).
- `configs/soft_reward_baseline_s2.yaml` (new): standalone S2 config for the soft-reward baseline agent.
- `tests/test_attacking_provider.py` (new, 6 tests), `tests/test_harness.py` (+5), `tests/test_environment.py` (+3).
- Two real, checkpointed training runs (`checkpoints/dqn_s2.pt`, `checkpoints/soft_reward_baseline_s2.pt`, both gitignored) and two scratchpad sweep scripts (primary + delayed-onset supplementary; not committed, same status as every prior session's own scratchpad training/sweep scripts).

**Full test results: 568 passed, 1 xfailed** (554 prior + 14 new; zero regressions in any pre-existing test).

**What's working:** Part 1's attack-wrapper + dual-tracking measurement is real, tested, and proven correct in isolation (hand-verified synthetic case) and against real masking/floor computation (the harness-level divergence test). The real S5 sweep ran on real, freshly-trained S2 checkpoints for both agents, with real spread across 5 eval seeds, and a served-tier histogram, exactly as scoped.
**What's broken / incomplete:** the masked agent's `V(pi)` is genuinely nonzero at alpha>=0.5, most sharply at alpha>=0.9 (`0.30`) — **investigated to a confident, mechanistic conclusion this session** (not a masking-code bug; a real, now-quantified boundary of the one-way ratchet's defense against an attack strong enough to suppress the sensor's very first true reading), but this is a genuine divergence from this thread's own pre-registered "flat zero everywhere" prediction and changes how the headline result should be framed in the paper (see PROGRESS.md). The soft-reward agent's predicted clean monotonic rise also did not materialize (flat-then-step, same shape as the masked agent's own curve).
**Blockers:** none.
**Next session will:** per updated PROGRESS.md — integrate these real numbers into the paper draft's Limitations section (citing this session's specific figures) in place of any placeholder framing; the posture-saturation fix, the real LSTM forecaster, and Dashboard v2 remain the standing lower-priority items. A possible mitigation direction (e.g. a conservative startup/default floor, or detecting suspiciously low sensor variance as a tell that the input channel itself may be compromised) is flagged as **future work only** — not built this session, for whoever next picks up masking-adjacent work.
**Hard Rules check:** Hard Rule 2 — the property under direct test all session, and the one this session's central finding is about: verified `compute_mask`/`PolicyTable`'s own guarantee holds exactly (0 attacked-floor violations, every seed, every alpha) even where the stronger, TRUE-posture-referenced `V(pi)` does not stay at zero — the distinction between "the mask enforces what it's told" (holds) and "the ratchet can always recover the truth regardless of attack strength" (does not, past a measured threshold) is the whole finding, reported honestly rather than conflated. Hard Rule 3 — `AttackingForecastProvider` needed zero changes to `env/masking.py`/`agents/dqn.py`/`agents/soft_reward_baseline.py` (verified via `git diff --stat`); the one `env/environment.py` change was narrow, precedented, flagged, signed off, and proven backward-compatible before use. Hard Rule 7 — central to this session throughout: the masked agent's nonzero `V(pi)` was investigated with the same rigor as Part 1's own tests rather than dismissed or silently averaged away, the soft-reward agent's non-clean curve shape is reported as measured rather than smoothed into the paper's anticipated story, and the supplementary sweep is explicitly labeled secondary/confirmatory, never used to override the primary numbers. Hard Rule 1 — not touched, no reward code involved. `env/contracts.py`, `env/masking.py`, `agents/dqn.py`, `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py`, `attack/trace_generator.py`'s existing functions: all confirmed untouched (`git diff --stat`).
