### [SOLO — adversarial trace generator (steering attack, eq. 7)] — 2026-08-25 — main

**Branch sync confirmed before starting:** `git rev-parse main dev21` both `36f3b2092be0a9f773a623972b23abcee4729f93`.

**Session goal:** build the attack generator (`attack/trace_generator.py`) implementing the paper draft's equation 7 input-shaping attack, and prove -- with real, end-to-end tests through the real forecaster and masking code, not assumptions from the formula -- that it genuinely lowers estimated posture while masking's safety guarantee holds no matter how wrong the posture it's given is. Explicitly out of scope, per instruction: running the actual S5 dose-response sweep (a separate, later session).

**Read first:** PROGRESS.md's "Next task" (confirmed the attack generator is the last remaining piece before the dose-response sweep, and that both agents' unmanipulated baselines are already measured on S1/S3); SESSION_LOG.md's last four entries (p99_latency root-cause, masked-vs-soft-reward S3 comparison -- memorized the real numbers: masked below-floor rate `0.0000` vs. soft-reward `0.1687` -- and the soft-reward agent build); PLAN.md Section 5's S5 row and Hard Rule 2 exactly ("Floors are enforced by action masking in the environment, not by penalties. Threat signals may only *raise* floors."); PLAN2.md Section 7.5 (Panel 5's dose-response framing, "security that isn't in the reward can't be steered out of it, because it was never a preference to begin with"); `env/forecast_provider.py` in full (`MovingAverageForecaster`, `ForecastProvider` interface); `env/masking.py`'s `compute_mask()`/`PolicyTable.floor()`/the floor table; `dashboard/explain.py` (read, not modified -- confirmed below).

**Design decisions (stated and justified, per instruction):**
1. **What `g(xt)` computes.** The real learned feature space (the LSTM forecaster, Addition A) doesn't exist -- only `env/forecast_provider.py`'s placeholder `MovingAverageForecaster`, driven by `env/environment.py::_threat_features_placeholder()`'s real `[qber, load]` window, does. Faithful interpretation for THIS placeholder: `g` doesn't need to be learned, it needs to be deterministic and to read as low threat when the current real forecaster processes it. Chose `g(xt) = [0.0] * len(xt)` -- zero qber (no bit-error/eavesdropping signal) and zero load (no backlog) are the actual lowest legitimate values either current placeholder feature can take, i.e. the real floor of this feature space's benign region, not an arbitrary pick. Documented explicitly in the module docstring: this targets the CURRENT placeholder forecaster and will need revisiting once the real LSTM forecaster exists -- expected, not a flaw.
2. **Interception point.** `generate_adversarial_window(true_window, alpha) -> shaped_window` is a pure function over `ForecastObservation["threat_features"]`'s real type (`Sequence[float]`). It does not touch `ForecastProvider`/`MovingAverageForecaster`/`environment.py` at all -- a future sweep session substitutes the shaped window for the true one before it reaches `forecaster.update()`. Verified this holds: `git diff --stat` on `env/contracts.py`, `env/forecast_provider.py`, `env/masking.py`, `env/environment.py`, `agents/dqn.py`, `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py` is empty after this session.
3. **File/naming.** `attack/trace_generator.py` (matching PROGRESS.md's and PLAN2.md's naming), new file -- the pre-existing `attack/steering_trace.py` stub (an older, PLAN.md-era name for the full dose-response module) was left untouched; not in scope this session.

**What got done:**
- `attack/trace_generator.py` (new): `g(window) -> list[float]` (all-zero window, same length) and `generate_adversarial_window(true_window, alpha) -> list[float]` implementing equation 7 exactly, `alpha` a required, independent, per-call parameter (not config-baked) so a future sweep can call it across eleven alpha values against the same base window in one run.
- `tests/test_trace_generator.py` (new, 23 tests): boundary correctness (alpha=0/1 exact equality, not approximate), linearity (alpha in {0.25, 0.5, 0.75} checked against an independently-recomputed formula in the test, `x + alpha*(g-x)`, not a re-call of the implementation), a no-mutation/new-object check, and two real-mechanism blocks below.

**Attack-effectiveness, verified end-to-end through the real forecaster (not assumed):** `env/forecast_provider.py`'s real placeholder formula has a documented structural ceiling (`tests/test_forecast_provider.py`'s posture-saturation investigation, 2026-08-24): `squashed_signal = sigmoid(mean([qber, load]))` is bounded in `[0.5, ~0.731]` for any real, in-domain `[qber, load]` pair in `[0,1]^2`, which the RBF-softmax always resolves to ELEVATED -- never HIGH, and (since `squashed_signal` can never drop below `0.5`) never CALM either. Verified directly against the real code before writing any assertion (scratchpad script, real `MovingAverageForecaster` instance, 60 EWMA updates to steady state at `alpha=0.3`): ordinary `[qber, load]` values genuinely can't cross a discrete posture bucket under this attack, matching the prior session's finding exactly. So this session's end-to-end test uses a genuinely severe `true_window = [5.0, 5.0]` -- the same way `env/environment.py`'s own real S2 `elevated_signal` mechanism is documented to reach HIGH ("a scripted signal unbounded by `[0, 1]`") -- to exercise the full mechanism, including a real discrete crossing:
- Real numbers (verified by direct execution, not derived on paper): `true_window=[5.0,5.0]` steady-states to `threat_score=0.9933`, `posture_probs=[0.0012, 0.1647, 0.8341]` (argmax HIGH). Shaped at `alpha=1.0` (full attack, `g=[0,0]`), it steady-states to `threat_score=0.5000` exactly, `posture_probs=[0.1371, 0.7258, 0.1371]` (argmax ELEVATED) -- a genuine bucket crossing, not just a lower number.
- `threat_score` decreases strictly monotonically across `alpha in {0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0}` (`0.9933 -> 0.9770 -> 0.9241 -> 0.7773 -> 0.5000`); `posture_probs[HIGH]` falls monotonically (`0.834 -> ... -> 0.137`) and `posture_probs[CALM]` rises monotonically (`0.0012 -> ... -> 0.1371`) across the same grid.

**Masking-safety, this session's most important test, verified with real numbers:** picked `sensitivity_class=S2`, where the HIGH->ELEVATED drop above genuinely changes the floor (`_PLACEHOLDER_FLOOR_TABLE`: S2+ELEVATED -> SERVE_PQC, S2+HIGH -> SERVE_HYBRID). `true_floor = SERVE_HYBRID`, `shaped_floor = SERVE_PQC` -- a real, concrete divergence, not a synthetic pair. `compute_mask()` called with only `shaped_floor` (the attacked, underestimated value) confirms: `SERVE_CLASSICAL` illegal (never below the floor the mask actually computed, wrong or not); `SERVE_PQC` legal -- the attack's real, measured effect, since the same call with `true_floor` shows `SERVE_PQC` illegal there. This is the concrete mechanism: an underestimated posture changes WHAT gets served (weaker tiers become legal), but never produces an action illegal relative to the (possibly wrong) floor the mask was actually given, because `compute_mask` has no separate notion of "the truth" to violate -- exactly why the masked agent's `V(pi)=0` guarantee (paper eq. 4) survives even a fully successful input-shaping attack. Also added a general, exhaustive form of the same property (parametrized over all 4 sensitivity classes x 3 postures = 12 real floor-table cells, independent of this session's specific numbers): for any posture handed to `PolicyTable.floor()`, `compute_mask()`'s output never legalizes anything below the resulting floor.

**`dashboard/explain.py` verified, not modified:** read in full. Every field it returns is sourced live from `compute_mask()`/the real environment/masking objects at decision time (module docstring: "structurally unable to drift... since they come from the same function call, not a parallel reimplementation"). A shaped input flowing through the real forecaster would produce a genuinely lower `threat_score`/`posture_probs`/`floor`, and `explain_decision_from_env` would report exactly that lowered chain consistently -- confirmed by reading the source, not just assumed, since it recomputes from the live env/forecaster state rather than caching or re-deriving anything.

**Full test results: 554 passed, 1 xfailed** (531 prior + 23 new, `tests/test_trace_generator.py`). `git status --porcelain` confirms only the two new files exist; `git diff --stat` on every protected file (`env/contracts.py`, `env/forecast_provider.py`, `env/masking.py`, `env/environment.py`, `agents/dqn.py`, `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py`, and every `reward.*`/`soft_reward.*` config block) is empty.

**What's working:** the attack generator is real, tested, and proven correct in isolation -- boundary/linearity/no-mutation contracts hold exactly, its effect on the real forecaster's output is measured and monotonic, and the masking-safety property it depends on is verified directly with real numbers, not assumed from Hard Rule 2's text.
**What's broken / incomplete:** the S5 dose-response sweep itself (both agents across eleven alpha values, reporting `V(pi)`) does not exist yet -- explicitly out of scope this session, per instruction.
**Blockers:** none.
**Next session will:** run the actual S5 dose-response sweep -- masked DQN vs. soft-reward baseline on S3 (or S5's own config, TBD that session) across eleven alpha values in `[0,1]`, reporting `V(pi)` (below-floor service rate, `experiments/harness.py::MultiSeedEvalResult.below_floor_rate_mean`, already built) for both policies, with the explicit paper prediction (soft-reward rises, masked stays flat at zero) confirmed or honestly reported otherwise per Hard Rule 7.
**Hard Rules check:** Hard Rule 2 -- the property under test all session: verified directly, with real numbers, that an underestimated posture only ever produces an underestimated floor, never an illegal one, rather than assuming the formula guarantees it. Hard Rule 3 -- the generator needed zero changes to `env/environment.py`/`env/masking.py`/`env/forecast_provider.py`, confirmed via `git diff --stat`, not just by not having touched them consciously. Hard Rule 1 -- not touched, no reward code involved. No temptation to fix the pre-existing posture-ceiling finding (an already-flagged, standing open item, out of scope) encountered -- it was instead used deliberately (a severe out-of-domain `true_window`, mirroring S2's own documented mechanism) to still get a real discrete-posture-crossing test rather than settling for a weaker continuous-only demonstration.

### [SOLO — root-cause p99_latency saturation on S3, diagnostic] — 2026-08-25 — main

**Branch sync confirmed before starting:** `git rev-parse main dev21` both `717355e4391c5988971096263e4869b13b725280`.

**Session goal:** root-cause the p99_latency==1.5000 saturation oddity, flagged unresolved across two prior sessions (Gate W3 S3 attempt: 6/6 policy-seed cells tied; masked-vs-soft-reward S3 comparison: 5/6 tied, one genuine exception at soft-reward seed=1's `1.2191`) -- diagnostic session, per standing convention: investigate first, fix only if a genuine defect is found, otherwise document honestly. The training-seed-variance finding from the prior session was explicitly out of scope and not touched.

**Read first:** PROGRESS.md's "Next task", SESSION_LOG.md's Gate W3 S3 attempt and masked-vs-soft-reward S3 comparison entries, `env/environment.py`'s `_LATENCY_UNITS`/`_apply_action` (the only place per-decision latency is computed -- confirmed exactly 4 discrete values: REUSE 0.2, SERVE_CLASSICAL 1.0, SERVE_PQC 1.2, SERVE_HYBRID 1.5, no cap/ceiling anywhere in this path), `env/deferral_queue.py` (confirmed `DeferralQueue.tick()`'s `steps_waited` feeds only the `DeferredCriticalStep` event log, never the `latencies` list `p99_latency` is computed from -- deferral wait time does not contribute to latency at all), `experiments/harness.py::run_scenario` (confirmed `p99_latency = np.percentile(latencies, 99)` over exactly one value per decision, appended via `_resolved_cost_action`/`_LATENCY_UNITS`, `latencies` length == `max_steps` == 250 per episode), `metrics/regret.py` (confirmed no separate/inconsistent latency computation exists there).

**The exact mechanism, confirmed against real data, not just derived analytically:** wrote a diagnostic script (scratchpad, not committed) that reloaded all six real checkpoints from the last session (`checkpoints/s3_{masked,soft_reward}_seed{1,4,7}.pt`) and, for every one of the 8 fixed eval seeds (900-907) per checkpoint (48 real episodes total), tallied the exact `cost_action` distribution feeding `latencies` alongside the reported `p99_latency`. Result: `np.percentile`'s default linear interpolation on a 250-length array reads index `(250-1)*0.99 = 246.51` -- interpolating between the 246th and 247th smallest values (0-indexed). Since `1.5` (SERVE_HYBRID) is the maximum of the 4-value discrete set, **whenever >= `250-246 = 4` of the 250 decisions cost SERVE_HYBRID (>=1.6% of the episode), both interpolation endpoints are `1.5`, so `p99_latency` reports exactly `1.5000`.** Verified exactly, not approximately: every one of the 48 real episodes with SERVE_HYBRID count >= 4 reported `1.5000` precisely; the single episode landing exactly at the boundary (soft-reward seed=1, eval_seed=901, SERVE_HYBRID count == 3) reported `1.3530`, matching `np.percentile([1.0]*246 + [1.2] + [1.5]*3, 99) == 1.3529999999999973` to 4 decimal places, confirmed by direct computation.

**The one-exception cell (soft-reward seed=1, `1.2191` from the prior session) is now understood down to the last decimal, not just attributed qualitatively:** across its own 8 eval seeds, this checkpoint's SERVE_HYBRID count is 0-2 for 7 of the 8 seeds (all reporting `1.2000`) and exactly 3 for eval_seed=901 (reporting the interpolated `1.3530`, the boundary case above) -- mean of these 8 values is `(1.2*7 + 1.3530)/8 = 1.219125`, matching the previously-reported `1.2191` exactly. This checkpoint is the sole exception because it rarely ever establishes a HYBRID-tier key at all (consistent with its already-noted near-total forced-rekey ratio `0.982` and highest below-floor rate `0.2555` -- it mostly REUSEs or serves cheaper tiers), unlike every other policy/seed combination tested (masked DQN: 10.4-18.8% SERVE_HYBRID at every seed; soft-reward seeds 4 and 7: 38.8-39.6% and 5.6-7.2% respectively), all of which clear the ~1.6% threshold comfortably.

**Verdict: NOT A BUG.** `1.5` is SERVE_HYBRID's real, uncapped per-decision cost -- there is no ceiling, cap, or timeout anywhere in the latency-computation path (confirmed by reading `env/environment.py::_apply_action` and `env/deferral_queue.py` in full) silently overriding a different, more-informative value. The saturation is a mechanical, fully-explained artifact of computing a 99th-percentile statistic over a series with only 4 possible values, compounded by S3's scarcity-driven floor making >=1.6% SERVE_HYBRID decisions typical for essentially any real trained policy on that scenario. This makes `p99_latency` a genuinely low-information discriminator specifically under scarcity scenarios like S3 (not broken, just coarse) -- consistent with, and now fully mechanistically explains, the exact phenomenon both prior sessions flagged but left unresolved.

**What got done (documentation only, per the diagnostic outcome -- no fix, no reward/masking/latency-model/agent code touched):**
- `experiments/harness.py`: `ScenarioResult.p99_latency`'s docstring gained the exact mechanism above (interpolation index formula, the >=4/250 threshold, the 48-episode verification, and a pointer to `total_reward`/`below_floor_rate` as sharper alternatives for S3 comparisons). `MultiSeedEvalResult.p99_latency_mean`/`_std` gained a matching, shorter cross-reference note. No field, function, or existing test changed.
- A diagnostic script (scratchpad, not committed) reloading all six real S3 checkpoints and tallying per-episode `cost_action` distributions against reported `p99_latency`, confirming the mechanism against real, already-trained weights rather than synthetic data alone.

**Full test results: 531 passed, 1 xfailed** -- identical to before this session (docs-only change, confirmed no regression).

**What's working:** the p99-latency-saturation oddity, flagged unresolved across two prior sessions, is now fully root-caused with an exact, numerically-verified mechanism -- both the general 5/6-or-6/6 saturation pattern and the specific one-exception-cell value (`1.2191`) are explained down to the last decimal.
**What's broken / incomplete:** nothing new -- this was a closed-loop diagnostic with a clean "not a bug" outcome. `p99_latency` remains genuinely coarse on scarcity scenarios by construction (a documented, not fixed, property); callers comparing policies on S3 should prefer `total_reward`/`below_floor_rate`.
**Blockers:** none.
**Next session will:** per updated PROGRESS.md -- return to the attack generator (`attack/trace_generator.py`) and the S5 dose-response sweep, now unblocked (this diagnostic's own resolution, either way, was the last named precondition).
**Hard Rules check:** Hard Rule 7 -- the central discipline this session ran on: followed the evidence to a "not a bug" conclusion rather than forcing a fix to feel more complete, and reported the one-exception cell's exact mechanism rather than leaving it as a qualitative hand-wave. Hard Rules 1, 2, 9 -- not touched: no reward, masking, or deferral logic read this session was modified (only docstrings). The training-seed-variance finding from the prior session was explicitly not investigated or touched, per instruction. `env/contracts.py`, `configs/default.yaml`/`configs/soft_reward_baseline.yaml`/`configs/soft_reward_baseline_s3.yaml`'s `reward.*`/`soft_reward.*`, `agents/dqn.py`, `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py`, `env/masking.py`, `env/environment.py`: all confirmed untouched (only `experiments/harness.py` gained docstring text, no logic change).

### [SOLO — masked DQN vs soft-reward baseline on real S3] — 2026-08-25 — main

**Branch sync confirmed before starting:** `git rev-parse main dev21` both `6697df90d9766066c158873aafc0dbb2d181259e`.

**Session goal:** PROGRESS.md's "Next task" follow-up (1) — a direct, unmanipulated comparison of the masked DQN against the soft-reward baseline agent on real S3, using the same checkpoint-averaged/multi-seed methodology Gate W3 established, to replace the paper draft's flagged placeholder Table V numbers with real measurements. No adversarial signal shaping (attack generator explicitly out of scope, per instruction) — both agents run against S3 exactly as it already exists.

**Read/verified first, findings before running anything:**
- `ls checkpoints/`: only S1-era checkpoints (`dqn_s1.pt`, four `dqn_s1_spike_sweep_*.pt` from the 2026-08-10 load-spike thread, `soft_reward_baseline_s1.pt` from last session) exist — **no reusable masked-DQN S3 checkpoint from the Gate W3 S3 session survived** (that session's own log entry confirms why: it used an uncommitted scratchpad script specifically because `train()` hardcoded `scenario: "S1"` at the time). Confirmed, not assumed, per instruction to check first: **both agents needed fresh training campaigns on S3 this session.**
- `configs/scenarios/s3_degradation.yaml` (read in full): the real, recalibrated S3 config Gate W3's S3 half used — `pool.capacity_bits: 20_000`, `qkd_degradation.spike_skr_multiplier: 0.0`. Directly reusable for the masked agent via `train(load_full_config(...), scenario="S3", ...)` — no new masked-agent config needed.
- `configs/soft_reward_baseline.yaml` (read in full, from last session): `scenario: S1` only, no `qkd_degradation`/recalibrated `pool` keys — confirmed it would raise `KeyError` under `scenario="S3"` (S3 dispatch requires `qkd_degradation`, per `env/environment.py::__init__`'s fail-fast convention). A new, standalone S3 variant was needed — exactly the gap PROGRESS.md's follow-up (1) itself named.
- `experiments/harness.py::evaluate_multi_seed`/`MultiSeedEvalResult` (read in full): already reports `p99_latency`, `total_reward`, `forced_rekey_ratio`, `regret_events`, `pool_exhaustion_events`, and `floor_violations_total` as mean/std (or summed, for the last) across eval seeds — four of Table V's five columns already covered by existing, tested code. The fifth (below-floor **service rate**, equation 4's numerator over its denominator) was NOT already present as a rate — `floor_violations_total` is a summed raw count, not normalized per episode.
- `env/masking.py::compute_mask()` / `experiments/harness.py::run_scenario`'s own `floor_violations` counter (re-read, per instruction point 8): confirmed `floor_violations` already IS "count of decisions whose delivered tier fell below the floor that would have applied" (via `_delivered_tier(action, key_type_onehot, floor)` compared against the real per-decision floor) — not "count of illegal actions", which wouldn't even be a coherent concept for the soft-reward agent (it has no masking to violate). This is exactly the same evidence-gathering approach the soft-reward agent's own build session already used (`ScenarioResult.floor_violations`), reused here, not reinvented.
- `experiments/train.py::train()`/`train_soft_reward_baseline()` (re-confirmed): both take a real `scenario` parameter (an external override, not read from `full_config["scenario"]`) and both accept `training_overrides["seed"]` — the exact interface Gate W3's own methodology depends on.
- `metrics/regret.py` (re-read): `EpisodeMetrics.regret_events`/`forced_rekey_ratio` computed exactly as expected (deferral-onset count; forced/total rekeys) — confirms no approximation needed, these are pulled from real code via `ScenarioResult.episode_metrics`.
- No paper PDF found in the repo (`find . -iname "*paper*"` — no hits); this session's brief reproduced the relevant Table V framing directly, treated as authoritative in its place.

**Design decisions, argued explicitly:**
1. **New standalone config, not a merged-at-runtime dict**: `configs/soft_reward_baseline_s3.yaml` (new file) — `pool:`/`qkd_degradation:` copied verbatim from the real, committed `configs/scenarios/s3_degradation.yaml` (S3's own recalibration, not re-derived or re-tuned here — this is a measurement session); `security_masking: false`/`soft_reward:` copied verbatim from `configs/soft_reward_baseline.yaml`. Matches this repo's existing one-standalone-file-per-scenario convention (`configs/scenarios/s2_hndl.yaml` etc.) rather than inventing a runtime-merge mechanism — the same choice PROGRESS.md's own follow-up (1) language anticipated ("a parallel S3 config... would need `configs/soft_reward_baseline.yaml`-style S3 dispatch keys added").
2. **`evaluate_multi_seed`/`MultiSeedEvalResult` gained `below_floor_rate_mean`/`below_floor_rate_std`** (additive fields, no existing field changed, no existing call site broken — confirmed no other file constructs `MultiSeedEvalResult` directly). Computed as `floor_violations / effective_max_steps` per seed, then mean/std across seeds — `effective_max_steps` resolved via `config.get("max_steps", _DEFAULT_MAX_STEPS)`, the *same* fallback `run_scenario` itself uses for every one of `evaluate_multi_seed`'s own calls, not a separately-guessed denominator. Verified this denominator is exact, not assumed: `run_scenario`'s outer loop increments `total_requests`/calls `env.step()` exactly once per iteration, and `SmartKeyNetEnv.step()` truncates at exactly `_decision_count >= max_steps` (checked right after incrementing) — regret/deferral events are absorbed entirely inside `_advance_to_next_decision`'s internal loop and never reach the outer `run_scenario` loop as a separate iteration, so `total_requests == max_steps` deterministically, for every seed, regret events or not. This is genuinely reusable code (any future policy/scenario comparison, including the eventual S5 dose-response sweep, will want this same rate) — built and tested to the same standard as `evaluate_multi_seed` itself was in the Gate W3 session, not a one-off script computation.
3. **Training budget: 75,000 steps/seed, matching Gate W3's S3 methodology exactly, not the config files' own 25,000-step default** — per instruction to use "the SAME rigor and methodology, not a lighter one." Calibrated first (3,000-step timed runs for both agents' S3 configs: masked 9.76s → ~244s/seed extrapolated; soft-reward 7.72s → ~193s/seed extrapolated), then ran the full 3-seed x 75,000-step campaign for both agents.
4. **Periodic eval snapshots suppressed during training** (`eval_every` set to `total_steps`, i.e., only the final checkpoint is snapshotted) — this session only needs each seed's *final* trained checkpoint for the real, separate 8-eval-seed `evaluate_multi_seed` pass; skipping the ~30 mid-run snapshots `train()`/`train_soft_reward_baseline()` would otherwise take saves real wall time without affecting the final checkpoint's weights (training itself is identical either way — `eval_every` only gates when `run_scenario` gets called for logging, never touches `agent.observe()`/`agent.learn()`).

**A real scratchpad-script mishap, disclosed rather than silently smoothed over**: the first run's script (mirroring the Gate W3 S3 session's own "uncommitted scratchpad training script" convention) crashed on writing its results JSON (a relative-path bug, `scratchpad/results.json` resolved against the repo-root cwd rather than the actual scratchpad directory) *after* both agents' full 3-seed training campaigns had already completed and been checkpointed to `checkpoints/s3_{masked,soft_reward}_seed{1,4,7}.pt`. Rather than re-running the expensive ~21-minute training campaign, a second script reloaded the six already-trained checkpoints directly (`DQNAgent(...); agent.load(path)`) and re-ran only the cheap `evaluate_multi_seed` calls (8 short eval episodes each) to recover the full summary — verified byte-for-byte identical to the first run's own (successfully-printed, pre-crash) per-seed log lines for every metric, confirming the reload correctly reproduced the exact trained weights, not a divergent re-run.

**Full real numbers, checkpoint-averaged (3 training seeds x 8 eval seeds = 24 eval episodes per agent, `eval_max_steps=250`), reported exactly as measured (Hard Rule 7):**

| Metric | Masked DQN (mean ± training-seed std) | Soft-reward baseline (mean ± training-seed std) |
|---|---|---|
| p99_latency | 1.5000 ± 0.0000 | 1.4064 ± 0.1324 |
| total_reward (env's own masked-style reward, both agents' trajectories scored under the SAME formula for apples-to-apples comparison — **not** what the soft-reward agent itself trains against) | -10214.82 ± 1303.25 | -11010.19 ± 12380.91 |
| forced_rekey_ratio | 0.156 ± 0.037 | 0.703 ± 0.408 |
| regret_events (== pool_exhaustion_events, same count by construction — see `experiments/harness.py`'s own docstring) | 0.00 ± 0.00 | 3.54 ± 5.01 |
| **below_floor_rate** (new metric, this session) | **0.0000 ± 0.0000** | **0.1687 ± 0.0759** |
| floor_violations_total (summed, all 24 episodes) | 0 | 1012 |

Per-training-seed breakdown (the raw evidence the table above is averaged from, never discarded):

| | seed=1 | seed=4 | seed=7 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Masked DQN** p99 / reward / frr / regret / below_floor_rate | 1.5000 / -8373.50 / 0.196 / 0.00 / 0.0000 | 1.5000 / -11065.98 / 0.107 / 0.00 / 0.0000 | 1.5000 / -11204.99 / 0.165 / 0.00 / 0.0000 |
| **Soft-reward** p99 / reward / frr / regret / below_floor_rate | 1.2191 / -433.84 / 0.982 / 0.00 / 0.2555 | 1.5000 / -28382.90 / 0.126 / 10.62 / 0.1800 | 1.5000 / -4213.82 / 1.000 / 0.00 / 0.0705 |

(Masked DQN's per-seed numbers are byte-for-byte identical to the Gate W3 S3 session's own reported values — `-8373.50`/`-11065.98`/`-11204.99`, `0.196`/`0.107`/`0.165` — a real, unplanned reproducibility check: same seeds, same config, same code, same result, confirming nothing has drifted since that session.)

**The honest read, per Hard Rule 7 — this session's central finding, and it does NOT match the paper draft's anticipated tradeoff shape:**

The below-floor divergence is exactly as expected and is the headline, structural result: masked DQN's `0.0000` (0/6000 decisions across every seed) vs. soft-reward's `0.1687` (1012/6000, ranging 7-26% by training seed) is the direct, measured confirmation of Hard Rule 2's guarantee holding for the masked agent and genuinely NOT holding for the soft-reward reproduction — the entire point of building both agents.

**But the anticipated tradeoff — "soft-reward wins on latency/rekey-ratio efficiency, masked agent buys its safety guarantee with more conservative behavior" — did not materialize.** Investigated, not just reported once and dropped (Hard Rule 7: "investigate before defending the method if a result doesn't match the intended story"): the masked DQN comes out **ahead or comparable on every metric measured this session, not just below-floor rate**:
- **`forced_rekey_ratio`: masked (`0.156`, mostly proactive) is BETTER than soft-reward (`0.703`, mostly forced)** — the opposite of "soft-reward is more efficient at rekey timing." The soft-reward agent's reward has no rekey-cost term shaped by load at all (see `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py`'s reward formula) beyond the flat REUSE-is-cheap/tier-actions-cost-more structure, so once it grabs a tier it just holds it until forced — consistent with last session's own S1 finding (`forced_rekey_ratio == 1.000` at every S1 checkpoint), now confirmed to persist on S3 too (albeit with more seed-to-seed variance: `0.982`, `0.126`, `1.000`).
- **`regret_events`/`pool_exhaustion_events`: masked `0.00` vs. soft-reward `3.54` mean — a second, DISTINCT failure mode beyond below-floor service, not previously the headline story.** `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py::compute_soft_reward`'s formula (`r = -w_lat*latency - w_en*energy + w_sec*security_score(tier)`) has **no term at all** for pool scarcity or starvation (deliberately — it reproduces Noetzold's own, simpler formula, not "our formula plus a security term," per that session's own Hard Rule 1 tension resolution). On S1 (effectively unconstrained pool) this blind spot was invisible; on S3's genuinely scarce, recalibrated pool it becomes a real, measured liability — seed 4's soft-reward checkpoint alone produced a mean 10.62 regret events per 250-step eval episode (vs. masked DQN's 0.00 at every seed, every eval). This means the soft-reward agent doesn't just trade security for something else — on a scarcity-constrained scenario, it can fail on BOTH security (below-floor rate) AND availability (pool exhaustion) simultaneously, because its reward literally has no incentive against either failure mode. This is a genuinely different, richer story than "leaks security in exchange for efficiency," and is reported here as such rather than reframed to fit the paper draft's anticipated shape.
- **`total_reward` (masked-style yardstick) is close on average (`-10214.82` vs. `-11010.19`) but the soft-reward agent's variance across training seeds is nearly 10x larger (`1303.25` vs. `12380.91`)** — driven almost entirely by seed 4's real pool-exhaustion penalties. The masked agent is both safer and dramatically more consistent run-to-run; the soft-reward agent's outcome depends heavily on which training seed happened to avoid draining the pool.
- **`p99_latency` is close (`1.5000` vs. `1.4064`) and, per the caveat below, not a reliable discriminator regardless.**

**A plausible explanation, offered as interpretation, not re-verified by a controlled ablation this session**: the masked agent's full reward (`w_lat`/`w_en`/`w_fr`/`w_qkd`/`r_starve`/`c_rekey_base+beta*load`, PLAN.md §4) gives it incentives against exactly the failure modes S3 stresses — pool-bit cost and starvation penalties discourage over-drawing hybrid material, and load-scaled rekey cost discourages timing rekeys badly. The soft-reward agent's reproduced formula has none of that; its only incentive is a fixed per-decision latency/energy-vs-security tradeoff, blind to the environment's actual scarcity state. On S1 (pool never meaningfully stressed) this blind spot costs nothing measurable; on S3 it costs real availability, not just real security margin. Framed as a hypothesis consistent with the data, not asserted as proven — a genuine ablation (e.g. isolating whether it's the missing `w_qkd`/`r_starve` terms specifically, vs. simply "no masking lets behavior wander more broadly") would be future work, not attempted here.

**The previously-flagged, unresolved p99-latency-saturation oddity (Gate W3 S3 session) reappears here, but not universally this time**: masked DQN ties at exactly `1.5000` across all 3 training seeds (matching the Gate W3 session's own finding that this metric is non-discriminating for the masked agent); soft-reward ALSO ties at `1.5000` for 2 of its 3 seeds (4 and 7) — 5 of 6 policy-seed cells this session tie at the same value. **But soft-reward's seed 1 breaks the pattern with a genuinely different value, `1.2191`** — the first real evidence in this thread that `p99_latency` isn't universally saturated; it happens to coincide with seed 1 also being the soft-reward checkpoint with the HIGHEST below-floor rate (`0.2555`) and near-total forced-rekey ratio (`0.982`), consistent with that particular trained policy rarely establishing a HYBRID-tier key at all (mostly reusing/serving classical or PQC, which cost less than `1.5` — see `env/environment.py`'s `_LATENCY_UNITS`, where `1.5` is specifically `SERVE_HYBRID`'s cost). This is consistent with, not a re-verification of, the Gate W3 session's own reading ("`p99_latency` is coarse... constant across the entire threshold grid... confirmed non-discriminating") — still flagged as a real, still-not-root-caused property of this metric, not silently accepted as a coincidental tie.

**What got done:**
- `configs/soft_reward_baseline_s3.yaml` (new file): standalone S3 config for `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py`, as described above.
- `experiments/harness.py`: `MultiSeedEvalResult` gained `below_floor_rate_mean`/`below_floor_rate_std`; `evaluate_multi_seed` computes them. No other field/function changed.
- `tests/test_harness.py` (+2): a masked-policy zero-rate check, and a direct manual-computation cross-check using `security_masking: false` + an always-SERVE_CLASSICAL stub policy on S2 (mirrors `tests/test_environment.py`'s own established `security_masking` proof combination).
- `tests/test_train.py` (+2): `configs/soft_reward_baseline_s3.yaml` loads/constructs a working env with `security_masking is False`/`scenario == "S3"`; a short real training smoke run against it completes without crashing.
- Two scratchpad scripts (not committed, same status as the Gate W3 S3 session's own scratchpad training script): the training+eval campaign, and the checkpoint-reload+re-eval recovery script. Six real, saved checkpoints in `checkpoints/` (gitignored): `s3_masked_seed{1,4,7}.pt`, `s3_soft_reward_seed{1,4,7}.pt`.

**Full test results: 531 passed, 1 xfailed** (up from 527 passed, 1 xfailed — 4 new tests, zero other files' tests affected).

**What's working:** the real Table-V-shaped comparison this session set out to produce exists, with real numbers across all five metrics, checkpoint-averaged with spread reported (never a bare point estimate), matching Gate W3's own rigor exactly (same 3-training-seed x 8-eval-seed design). The below-floor structural guarantee (Hard Rule 2) is confirmed to hold on S3 too, not just S1.
**What's broken / incomplete:** none for this session's own scope. The paper draft's anticipated "soft-reward wins efficiency" framing does not hold on S3 — flagged explicitly above, not silently corrected in the write-up to fit the expected story.
**Blockers:** none.
**Next session will:** per updated PROGRESS.md — the attack generator (`attack/trace_generator.py`) and the S5 dose-response sweep, now that both agents being compared are real and their unmanipulated baseline behavior on both S1 and S3 is measured.
**Hard Rules check:** Hard Rule 7 — the central discipline this session ran on: the below-floor/regret/rekey-ratio/variance findings are reported exactly as measured, including the honest divergence from the paper draft's anticipated tradeoff shape and the still-unresolved p99-latency-saturation oddity (neither smoothed over nor treated as alarming). Hard Rule 1 — not touched: neither agent's reward function was modified; both trained via their existing, unmodified `train()`/`train_soft_reward_baseline()` entry points. Hard Rule 9 — not touched directly; the soft-reward agent's real regret/pool-exhaustion events on S3 were reported as found, not fixed (per instruction) — Hard Rule 9's deferral-not-downgrade guarantee itself was not re-verified this session (out of scope; that's `env/environment.py`'s own, already-tested mechanism, unmodified). `env/contracts.py`, `configs/default.yaml`'s `reward.*`, `configs/soft_reward_baseline.yaml`'s weights, `agents/dqn.py`, `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py`, `env/masking.py`, and `env/environment.py`: all confirmed untouched (only `experiments/harness.py` gained additive fields; the two new config files are genuinely new, not edits to protected ones).

### [SOLO — soft-reward baseline agent (Noetzold-style reproduction)] — 2026-08-25 — main

**Branch sync confirmed before starting:** `git rev-parse main dev21` both `ff9e5845007f3465d8d1e7370f97438d9b4bcd1e`.

**Session goal:** start (and only) `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py` — PROGRESS.md's standing open item since the very first session log entry — the Noetzold-style soft-reward baseline agent PLAN.md §5 S5's future steering attack needs as its target: security as a soft reward term, no action masking. The attack generator and the S5 dose-response sweep are explicitly out of scope this session, per instruction.

**Read first, findings before designing anything:**
- `PROGRESS.md`/`SESSION_LOG.md`'s last 3 entries: confirmed the full S1-S6 grid is real, Gate W3 is met on both halves, and `experiments/train.py::train()` now takes a real `scenario` parameter — all context only, nothing here modified.
- `PLAN.md` §4's reward formula and Hard Rules re-read carefully. `HANDOFF_C.md` does not exist in this solo-mode repo (confirmed, per standing memory note) — the reproduced spec from this session's brief was treated as authoritative in its place.
- `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py` (read in full): already existed as a stub — module docstring plus `soft_reward(state, action, threat_score) -> float` and a `SoftRewardAgent` class, every method `raise NotImplementedError`. Not a locked contract (PROGRESS.md only ever described this file as "not started"): this session's design below takes a different, more reuse-maximizing shape (see Design decision 1) and fully supersedes the stub's skeleton.
- `agents/dqn.py` (read in full): `DQNAgent`'s masking lives entirely in the `mask: ActionMask` argument passed into `act()`/`learn()` — the class itself has zero notion of a security floor, policy table, or `Action` tiers' relative ordering. This is the key finding that shaped Design decision 1 below.
- `env/masking.py::compute_mask()` (read in full, per instruction, treated read-only): confirmed the floor-based rule (`int(action) >= int(floor)`) and the REUSE-below-floor rule (gated on `current_key_type is not None`) are the only two rules keyed on the security floor; `pool_can_draw`/`key_age` gate SERVE_HYBRID/REUSE independently of floor.
- `env/environment.py::step()` (read in full): **found a real, non-obvious blocker, exactly the kind this session's brief asked to be stopped and flagged before working around** — `step()` unconditionally raises `IllegalActionError` for any action outside the current mask, regardless of which agent is calling. This means "no action masking" cannot be achieved solely by the new agent's own action-selection code ignoring the mask (as the brief's preferred design assumed) — the environment itself is the enforcer. Surfaced to the user via `AskUserQuestion` before writing any code; user selected the additive opt-in env-flag approach (see Design decision 2).
- `env/pool_sim.py::draw()`: confirmed `PoolExhaustedError` fires if a caller draws more than the pool holds — meaning a blanket "skip all masking" would let `_apply_action` crash the simulator on SERVE_HYBRID when the pool is empty. This ruled out a naive full mask bypass and shaped Design decision 2's narrower scope (floor-only, not feasibility).
- `env/contracts.py`'s `Action` enum confirmed: `SERVE_CLASSICAL=0 < SERVE_PQC=1 < SERVE_HYBRID=2` — the ordinal structure Design decision 2's `floor=Action.SERVE_CLASSICAL` trick relies on (every tier's own ordinal already clears the lowest floor).
- `experiments/harness.py::run_scenario` (read in full): calls `env.step(action)` directly and already computes `floor_violations` from each action's real *delivered* tier vs. the state's real `policy_floor` — this became the direct, reusable proof mechanism for "served below floor" (see Testing), needing zero harness.py changes.

**Hard Rule 1 tension, resolved explicitly (per instruction, before writing any reward code):** Hard Rule 1 ("no security term in the reward, ever") governs `agents/dqn.py` and `env/environment.py::_apply_action`'s own reward computation, absolutely — both remain completely clean of `security_score`/any equivalent, verified by a direct grep test (`tests/test_soft_reward_baseline.py`'s two Hard Rule 1 boundary tests), not assumed. `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py` is the deliberate, contained exception: a reproduction of a flawed prior-work design, built specifically so the flaw can be measured and attacked later — the reference artifact Hard Rule 1's own argument is tested against, not a violation of it. Its reward (`r = -w_lat*latency - w_en*energy + w_sec*security_score(tier)`) is the reproduced design's OWN, simpler formula — not "our full reward plus a security term bolted on," which would not be a genuine reproduction of Noetzold's design. `env/environment.py`'s own internal reward computation still runs every step (the environment can't skip its own accounting) but is never read by this agent's training loop — discarded explicitly, by construction, not by convention (see Design decision 3, and the poisoned-reward test below).

**Design decisions, argued explicitly:**
1. **Architecture reuse — no new agent class.** Once the masking difference was resolved to live in the environment's config (Design decision 2), the only genuine difference between the two agents is which reward value reaches `agent.observe()` — not how actions are selected, learned, or represented. `agents.dqn.DQNAgent` is therefore reused **directly and completely unmodified** (not subclassed, not composed) — `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py` contributes only the reward function (`compute_soft_reward`), its config (`SoftRewardConfig`/`load_soft_reward_config`), and tier-resolution helpers. This is the maximal-reuse point the brief asked for: zero duplication of `QNetwork`/replay buffer/training mechanics, and `agents/dqn.py` is provably untouched (`git diff` confirms).
2. **Where "no masking" lives (env/environment.py design decision 16, signed off before being built — see the AskUserQuestion above):** `SmartKeyNetEnv` gained `config.get("security_masking", True)`. When `False`, `_prepare_decision` calls the same, unmodified `compute_mask()` with `floor=Action.SERVE_CLASSICAL` (every tier's own ordinal clears the lowest floor, making the floor rule a no-op) and `current_key_type=None` (the REUSE-below-floor rule is gated on this being non-`None`, so it becomes a no-op too) — while `pool_can_draw`/`key_age`/`max_key_age` pass through unchanged, so pool-exhaustion and key-age-cap feasibility still apply (these are physical/protocol constraints, not the security-floor restriction this flag targets, and lifting them too would crash `pool_sim.draw()` or contradict Hard Rule 9). The real floor still drives `state["policy_floor"]`/`self._current_floor` regardless, so REKEY_NOW still resolves to at least the real floor's tier (a property of what REKEY_NOW *means* via `_resulting_key_type`, not a masking rule) and this agent's own reward function can still tell, after the fact, whether a decision landed below the floor it bypassed. Default `True` — every pre-existing config/caller unaffected, proven by a byte-for-byte regression test (masks and rewards identical across a full 200-step S2 episode with the key explicit-`True` vs. absent entirely). Zero `env/masking.py` changes.
3. **security_score provenance and the REUSE/REKEY_NOW mapping (Hard Rule 4):** `classical=0.2, pqc=0.6, hybrid=1.0` come directly from the reproduced spec, not invented fresh. `REUSE`/`REKEY_NOW` don't get their own constant — both resolve to whatever tier they actually *deliver* (`delivered_tier`, mirroring `experiments/harness.py::_delivered_tier`'s existing resolution: REUSE delivers the existing session tier unchanged — which, unlike the masked agent, can genuinely be below the real floor now that `compute_mask`'s REUSE-below-floor rule is a no-op; REKEY_NOW delivers `max(existing tier, floor)`, since that resolution is a property of what REKEY_NOW means in this environment, not a masking rule) — then scored via that tier's own entry in the same three-value table. Cost (latency/energy) uses a separate resolution (`resolved_cost_action`, mirroring `_resolved_cost_action`): REUSE costs against itself (a cheap cache hit, 0.2/0.1 units), never against the tier it happens to deliver.
4. **File/config naming:** `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py` (superseding the pre-existing stub) and `configs/soft_reward_baseline.yaml` (new, standalone — not a variant of `configs/default.yaml`, matching the instruction that this agent's reward weights "belong in their own config, not mixed into the masked agent's"). The new config's own `reward:`/`dqn:` blocks are copied verbatim from `configs/default.yaml` (the former inert — `SmartKeyNetEnv.__init__` needs it to construct but this agent's training loop never reads it; the latter genuinely identical, since "the DQN is identical in architecture" is meant literally here); only `security_masking: false` and the new `soft_reward:` block (`w_lat=1.0, w_en=0.1, w_sec=1.0` — `w_lat`/`w_en` matched to `default.yaml`'s own values for a comparable cost scale, `w_sec` sized to the same order of magnitude as a latency-unit cost so the trade-off is genuinely observable, not negligible or overwhelming) are new.
5. **Training entry point:** `experiments/train.py::train_soft_reward_baseline` — additive, does not touch `train()` (confirmed via `git diff`: `train()`'s own body is byte-for-byte unchanged). A parallel function, not a generalization of `train()`, because the one thing that differs — which reward reaches `agent.observe()` — isn't a parameterizable variant of `train()`'s existing shape, which is tightly coupled to using `env.step()`'s own returned reward (exactly what this agent must not train against). Lives in `experiments/train.py` rather than `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py` because it needs `GreedyDQNPolicy` (defined in `train.py`) and `run_scenario` (already imported there) — putting it in `agents/` would either duplicate `GreedyDQNPolicy` or invert this repo's existing layering (`experiments/` depends on `agents/`, never the reverse).

**Testing (real, direct proofs, not smoke tests):**
- **Full regression: 527 passed, 1 xfailed** (up from 506 passed, 1 xfailed at the start of this session — net +21: 4 new tests in `tests/test_environment.py`, 5 in `tests/test_train.py`, and `tests/test_soft_reward_baseline.py` rewritten from its single pre-existing import-smoke stub into 13 real behavioral tests, a net +12 for that file). Zero other files' tests changed.
- **`security_masking` default-True regression, byte-for-byte, not just "no crash"** (`test_security_masking_defaults_to_true_and_is_unaffected_by_the_keys_absence`): same S2 elevated-posture config/seed/`RandomPolicy`, every mask and every reward across a full 200-step episode compared directly between "`security_masking` key absent" and "explicit `True`" — identical in both cases.
- **The core "no masking" proof** (`test_security_masking_false_lets_a_policy_serve_below_the_real_floor`): same S2 config/seed, `RandomPolicy` under the default (`True`) mask → `floor_violations == 0` (as always); a stub policy that always attempts `SERVE_CLASSICAL` under `security_masking: false` → `floor_violations > 0`. Confirms `env/masking.py::compute_mask()`'s own behavior is unaffected (its entire pre-existing test suite, `tests/test_masking.py`, passes completely unmodified) — only which arguments `environment.py` passes to it differ.
- **Feasibility rules survive the flag** (`test_security_masking_false_still_gates_serve_hybrid_on_pool_and_reuse_on_key_age`): with `security_masking: false`, a scarce-pool config still masks `SERVE_HYBRID` exactly matching `pool_sim.can_draw()`'s real value, and a cold-start session (`key_age == max_key_age` by construction) still masks `REUSE` — both cross-checked against real env state, not hardcoded.
- **No-crash regression** (`test_security_masking_false_does_not_crash_a_full_random_valid_episode`): a full 300-step episode under `security_masking: false` with a mask-sampling random policy runs to completion via the unmodified `run_scenario` — no `IllegalActionError`, no `PoolExhaustedError`.
- **Reward function unit tests** (`tests/test_soft_reward_baseline.py`, 13 tests): `security_score` matches the spec's own values exactly; `delivered_tier`/`resolved_cost_action` resolve REUSE/REKEY_NOW correctly (including the below-floor REUSE case that distinguishes this agent from the masked one); `compute_soft_reward` matches the formula directly by hand-computed expected value, isolates the security term's sign/direction, and is proven independent of the real floor's value for direct tier actions; `load_soft_reward_config` reads the real committed file.
- **Hard Rule 1 boundary check, code-level** (`test_hard_rule_1_dqn_agent_contains_no_security_term`, `test_hard_rule_1_environment_apply_action_contains_no_security_term`): direct grep for `security_score`/`w_sec`/`TIER_SECURITY_SCORE`/`soft_reward` across `agents/dqn.py`'s whole file and `env/environment.py::_apply_action`'s body specifically (scoped the same way this repo's existing Hard-Rule-3 boundary check is, since the module docstring elsewhere legitimately discusses `security_masking` in prose) — zero hits in both.
- **Training-loop-level Hard Rule 1 proof** (`test_train_soft_reward_baseline_does_not_use_env_steps_own_reward`): monkeypatched `SmartKeyNetEnv.step` to return an impossible poison reward value (`-999999.0`); confirmed the replay buffer's stored transitions never contain it — direct proof `env.step()`'s own reward is genuinely discarded, not silently used.
- **Dispatch proof** (`test_train_soft_reward_baseline_uses_security_masking_false_environment`): monkeypatched `SmartKeyNetEnv.__init__` to capture every config passed to it during a real (short) training run; confirmed `security_masking is False` on every one (the training env plus each eval-snapshot env).
- **Hard Rule 8 guard, mirrored** (`test_train_soft_reward_baseline_respects_train_eligible_guard`): a soft-reward config with `train_eligible: false` still refuses to train, same convention as `train()`'s own guard.
- **The real, short training run required by this session's brief** (`test_train_soft_reward_baseline_trained_agent_serves_below_the_real_floor`, 3,000 steps): confirms loss trends down (second-half mean below first-half mean) and at least one eval snapshot has `floor_violations > 0` — both passed on the real committed config.

**Calibration:** 3,000 steps took 9.26s → extrapolated ~77.2s for the real, full 25,000-step campaign in `configs/soft_reward_baseline.yaml`'s own `training:` block.

**Full real training run (25,000 steps, S1, seed 0, the real committed config, not a test override) — the actual proof this session's goal was met, not inferred from the reward formula alone:**

Loss (24,937 real gradient steps once the replay buffer passed `batch_size=64`): starts high while `QNetwork` is untrained (first 5 values: `93.47, 26.98, 4.91, 7.74, 16.58`) and collapses to a low, noisy band within the first several dozen learn steps, settling around `0.15-0.25` for the remainder of the run (last 5 values: `0.205, 0.232, 0.158, 0.247, 0.184`; first-half mean `0.2048`, second-half mean `0.2136` — nearly flat *within* the settled band, because the collapse from ~93 to ~0.2 happens so early that a first-half/second-half split mostly compares two already-converged halves. Reported exactly as measured, per Hard Rule 7, rather than picking a split that flatters the trend.) The reward-window averages (this agent's own soft reward, 10 windows of 2,500 steps) trend clearly upward and turn positive: `-0.4061, -0.2408, -0.0571, 0.1094, 0.28, 0.4496, 0.516, 0.4581, 0.4573, 0.4107` — consistent with the agent learning to establish a HYBRID key once (a one-time cost of about `-0.66`/step under this config's weights) and then `REUSE` it repeatedly (worth about `+0.79`/step at the HYBRID security score, far cheaper than any fresh tier action), which also explains why every eval checkpoint's `forced_rekey_ratio` is exactly `1.000` (every rekey observed is a forced, key-age-cap rekey — the greedy policy never proactively rekeys once it holds a valuable tier).

Eval snapshots (`GreedyDQNPolicy`, every 2,500 steps, via the unmodified `run_scenario`, 250-step episodes) — **`floor_violations` is non-zero at every single one of the 10 checkpoints**, the direct, measured proof this agent genuinely trades security away:

**What got done:**
- `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py`: full implementation, superseding the prior `NotImplementedError` stub — `SoftRewardConfig`/`load_soft_reward_config`, `_TIER_SECURITY_SCORE`, `resolved_cost_action`/`delivered_tier`/`security_score`, `compute_soft_reward`.

- `configs/default.yaml`: `security_masking: true` added, documented, additive only.

- `tests/test_train.py` (+5): the soft-reward-baseline training-entry-point tests above.

**What's working:** the soft-reward baseline agent is real, tested, and its defining property (security is genuinely soft and gets traded away) is measured, not assumed — a real 25,000-step training campaign shows non-zero `floor_violations` at every eval checkpoint. Usable through `experiments/harness.py::run_scenario` with zero harness.py changes (proven by every test above that calls `run_scenario` directly with this agent's trained policy).
**What's broken / incomplete:** none for this session's scope. The attack generator and the S5 dose-response sweep remain not started, per instruction — explicitly out of scope this session.
**Blockers:** none.

Hard Rule 2 — deliberately, correctly absent for this agent only, via the signed-off `security_masking` flag; `env/masking.py` itself is completely unmodified and its own test suite passes unchanged. Hard Rule 4 — `security_score`'s three values come from the reproduced design's own spec, not invented; stated explicitly in Design decision 3. ### [SOLO — experiments/train.py accepts a real scenario parameter] — 2026-08-24 — main

**Branch sync confirmed before starting:** `git rev-parse main dev21` both `588b0f7baa4c346f93f2deec8d7fb2b00ddc4b25`.

**Session goal:** fix `experiments/train.py::train()`'s hardcoded `scenario: "S1"` override -- flagged three separate times across three prior sessions (the multi-seed eval probe, the S3 Gate W3 attempt's scratchpad workaround, and last session's S6 build) without being fixed -- and use the fix to finally exercise the Hard Rule 8 `train_eligible` guard through a real, scenario-selectable end-to-end `train()` call.

**Read first, findings before designing anything:**
- `experiments/train.py::train()` (read in full): exactly two hardcoded-`"S1"` sites, not more -- `env_config = {**full_config, "scenario": "S1", ...}` (the training env's own construction) and the periodic eval snapshot's `run_scenario(GreedyDQNPolicy(agent), "S1", eval_config, seed=eval_seed)`. No other S1-only assumption exists in this function: `terminated` is always `False` regardless of scenario (`env/environment.py` line 608, "no natural terminal state in this MDP", unconditional), so the continuous-training-loop design (`env.reset()` once, then `total_steps` `step()` calls with no mid-run reset) needed zero changes for any scenario. `evaluate_against_baseline()` (a separate function, not called by `train()`) hardcodes `"S1"` in three of its own call sites -- read, left untouched, out of this session's scope (it evaluates a *given, already-trained* agent, and the brief's tests only exercise `train()` directly).
- Grepped every caller of `train()` across `agents/`, `experiments/`, `tests/`: exactly two real call sites (`tests/test_train.py`, `experiments/train.py::main()`), neither passes a `scenario` argument today -- confirms a `scenario="S1"` default is sufficient for 100% backward compatibility, no wider blast radius exists.
- `experiments/harness.py::run_scenario(policy, scenario, config, seed)` (read in full): the existing convention is an **explicit, external `scenario` parameter that overrides whatever `config["scenario"]` itself says** (`env_config = {**config, "scenario": scenario, "seed": seed}`) -- not read automatically from the config dict. This is deliberate (the same `config` can be pointed at different scenarios by varying the argument alone) and is the convention `train()` needed to match, not invent a parallel one.
- `configs/default.yaml`/`configs/scenarios/s3_degradation.yaml`/`configs/scenarios/s6_migration.yaml` (read, not modified): each already carries its own top-level `scenario:` key (`S1`/`S3`/`S6` respectively) and a `training:` block (S6's present only for key-set completeness, since `train_eligible: false` blocks it regardless). `env/environment.py::__init__` reads `self._scenario = config.get("scenario", "S1")` directly off whatever config dict `SmartKeyNetEnv` is constructed with -- confirming that once `train()` threads an explicit `scenario` into `env_config`, no further `env/environment.py` change is needed; S4/S6-specific keys (`ddos.*`, `migration_graph_seed`/`migration_schedule`) are the caller's responsibility to have present in `full_config`, exactly like `run_scenario`'s existing contract.
- `tests/test_train.py` (read in full): 7 existing tests, none pass a `scenario` argument -- all must keep passing completely unmodified.

**Design decision, matching harness.py's convention exactly, not inventing a second one:** `train()` gained `scenario: str = "S1"` as a third parameter (after `full_config`, `training_overrides`) -- an explicit override, not read from `full_config["scenario"]`, mirroring `run_scenario`'s own signature and semantics precisely. Threaded into both hardcoded sites: the training env's `env_config` and the periodic eval snapshot's `run_scenario(...)` call (this second site was also fixed, even though the brief's literal ask was "the environment construction" -- flagged and fixed as an additional S1-only assumption per the brief's own instruction to look for more than one hardcoded line, since a training campaign selecting e.g. S3 should also see S3-reflective eval snapshots during training, not S1 ones). Defaulting to `"S1"` (not `full_config.get("scenario", "S1")`) is what makes every pre-existing call site behave byte-for-byte identically regardless of what a given config's own `scenario:` key says -- matching `run_scenario`'s own decoupling of "the config" from "which scenario it's run against."

**The Hard Rule 8 guard itself (`full_config.get("train_eligible", True)`, checked before any env/agent construction) was not touched, per instruction** -- it was already keyed on the config flag, not on scenario selection, so it didn't need to change; what changed is that it's now reachable through a call where `scenario` is genuinely, independently selectable, rather than a call that would have silently run S1 regardless of what was requested.

**Testing (real, direct proofs, not smoke tests):**
1. **Full regression**: all 7 pre-existing `tests/test_train.py` tests pass completely unmodified; full suite 506 passed, 1 xfailed (up from 501 passed, 1 xfailed -- 5 new tests, see below), zero other files touched (confirmed via `git status`/`git diff --stat`: only `experiments/train.py` and `tests/test_train.py` changed).
2. **Genuine before/after comparison, not just "tests pass"**: `git stash`'d this session's diff, ran `train(load_full_config(), training_overrides={"total_steps": 300, "eval_every": 100, "eval_max_steps": 50, ...})` (no `scenario` arg) against the real, unmodified pre-fix code, recorded exact values, `git stash pop`'d, and re-ran the identical call against the fixed code. **Byte-for-byte identical**: `reward_window_avgs` (`[-140.727734, -115.35223400000001, -117.97983400000001]`), full loss curve (237 real gradient steps, first `36966.12890625`, last `5178.3486328125`), and every eval snapshot's `scenario`/`total_reward`/`p99_latency`/`forced_rekey_ratio` (steps 100/200/300, e.g. `-2645.3858`/`1.5`/`0.5428571428571428` at both 100 and 300). Pinned as exact assertions in the new `test_train_default_scenario_is_byte_for_byte_identical_to_pre_fix_s1_behavior` test.
3. **New capability, direct proof `scenario="S3"` is load-bearing, not silently ignored** (`test_train_scenario_s3_dispatches_real_s3_degradation_dynamics`): (a) pointing `scenario="S3"` at `configs/default.yaml` (which lacks `qkd_degradation`, required only under real S3 dispatch in `env/environment.py`) raises `KeyError` -- proof the parameter genuinely reaches `SmartKeyNetEnv`'s dispatch, not a no-op; (b) training against the real, committed `configs/scenarios/s3_degradation.yaml` with `scenario="S3"` produces eval snapshots whose own `ScenarioResult.scenario` field reports `"S3"`. A second, mechanism-level test (`test_train_scenario_s3_pool_trajectory_genuinely_diverges_from_s1`) constructs the real env the same way `train()` does internally and runs a deterministic max-hybrid-draw stress test (mirrors SESSION_LOG.md's own 2026-08-24 S3-vs-S1 divergence methodology): S1's pool never drops below ~70% full (min fill `0.6974`, seed 0) while S3's collapses to near-total exhaustion (min fill `0.0016` = 32/20,000 bits, 33 regret events) -- exact match to the earlier S3 recalibration session's own numbers, confirming `train()`'s `scenario` parameter reaches the identical real mechanism, not a parallel path.
4. **The train_eligible guard, exercised for real, the single most important test this session produces** (`test_train_scenario_s6_guard_fires_before_any_env_or_training_work`): `train(s6_config, scenario="S6", ...)` raises `ValueError` mentioning `train_eligible` -- and, via a `monkeypatch`'d `SmartKeyNetEnv.__init__` call counter, confirmed the count stays exactly `0`: no environment is ever constructed, so no env step and no optimizer step occurred before the raise. This is the first time this guard has been proven to fire on a call where `scenario` was genuinely, independently selectable (previously it only ever fired because `train()` ignored whatever scenario a config named).
5. **Control test** (`test_train_scenario_s3_control_does_not_raise_guard_is_scenario_specific`): `train(s3_config, scenario="S3", ...)` (where `train_eligible` defaults `True`) does not raise and completes a real short training run -- confirms the guard is scenario-specific, not a blanket block on every non-S1 scenario.

**What got done:**
- `experiments/train.py`: `train()` gained `scenario: str = "S1"`, threaded into both the training env's `env_config` and the periodic eval snapshot's `run_scenario` call; docstrings (module-level and `train()`'s own) updated to describe the real, generalized behavior and retire stale "S1-only" framing. `evaluate_against_baseline()` and everything else in the file: untouched.
- `tests/test_train.py`: +5 new tests (the byte-for-byte before/after comparison, the S3 dispatch-proof pair, the S6 guard end-to-end proof, the S3 control test) -- all 7 pre-existing tests unmodified.

**What's working:** `train()`'s scenario is now a real, explicit, tested parameter, matching `experiments/harness.py`'s existing convention exactly; every pre-existing call site is unaffected (proven byte-for-byte); the Hard Rule 8 guard is now proven to work end-to-end through a genuinely scenario-selectable call, not just in isolation.
**What's broken / incomplete:** none for this session's scope. `evaluate_against_baseline()` remains S1-only -- flagged as a deliberate scope boundary, not a miss (it's a separate function evaluating an already-trained agent, not part of `train()`'s scenario-selection mechanism).
**Blockers:** none.
**Next session will:** per updated PROGRESS.md -- the standing items remain the posture-saturation flag (`env/forecast_provider.py`) and the not-yet-started soft-reward baseline agent / steering attack / dashboard / forecaster work. The soft-reward baseline agent's training work is now directly unblocked by this session's fix, since it will need to run on multiple scenarios.
**Hard Rules check:** Hard Rule 8 -- central to this session's proof, not its design (the guard's own logic/location was explicitly not touched, per instruction); verified it now fires through a real, scenario-selectable call, with an instrumented zero-environment-construction check proving it blocks before any real work, not just eventually. Hard Rule 3 -- checked per instruction: `scenario` selection surfaces no place where `train()` or environment construction leaks graph/tenant awareness into agent-facing code (the fix only threads an already-existing, already-scenario-agnostic parameter one level further; nothing new touches masking/reward/agent code). Hard Rules 1, 2, 9 -- untouched (no reward, masking, or deferral logic changed, confirmed via `git diff --stat` showing only `experiments/train.py`/`tests/test_train.py` changed). `env/contracts.py`, `configs/default.yaml`, `reward.*`, `agents/dqn.py`, `env/masking.py`, `env/environment.py`, every scenario config file's contents, and the `train_eligible` guard's own logic/location: all confirmed untouched.

### [SOLO — S6 (migration wave) scenario dispatch, Hard Rule 8 guard] — 2026-08-24 — main

**Branch sync confirmed before starting:** `git rev-parse main dev21` both `0c4f8bb59435696d0a83e2bae755df0fd8974522`.

**Session goal:** close PROGRESS.md Thread 3's last open item -- wire real S6 (migration wave) scenario dispatch, the final entry in the S1-S6 scenario grid, on top of the tenant-graph identity S4's session already built. Structurally enforce Hard Rule 8 (train/eval split for migration), not just document it.

**Read first (Step 0-9 of the session brief), findings before designing anything:**
- `env/masking.py::compute_mask`/`PolicyTable.floor` and `env/environment.py::_prepare_decision` both read `request["sensitivity_class"]` **fresh every decision**, with zero caching anywhere in the pipeline that assumes it's fixed for an episode (`floor = self._policy_table.floor(SensitivityClass(request["sensitivity_class"]), current_posture)`, recomputed every call). This meant S6 needed **zero masking.py changes** -- confirmed by reading before designing, exactly as the brief asked, not assumed.
- `env/request_generator.py::RequestGenerator._build_request` reads `attrs["sensitivity_class"]` off the tenant's node-attribute dict fresh on every call too -- the same property that made S4's `flood_override` purely additive makes S6 purely a request-generation-side mutation.
- `dashboard/explain.py` line ~291 (`sensitivity_class = SensitivityClass(request["sensitivity_class"])`) also reads fresh off the Request at decision time -- verified by reading, not assumed; no dashboard change needed.
- `experiments/train.py::train()` hardcodes `env_config = {**full_config, "scenario": "S1", ...}` (line ~130, the standing, now-three-times-flagged technical debt) -- **not fixed this session** (out of scope per instruction), but this meant a naive read might conclude S6 could never be trained on by accident anyway. Rejected that as the actual Hard Rule 8 mechanism: it's an accident of unrelated debt, not a real guard -- a future session fixing that hardcoding (to add real multi-scenario training) would silently defeat it. Built a real, explicit guard instead (see Design decisions).
- `experiments/harness.py::run_scenario`/`run_grid` are correctly scenario-agnostic ("any policy x any scenario") -- no guard belongs there; Hard Rule 8 is specifically about *training*, and eval-only usage of S6 is the scenario's entire purpose.

**Design decisions, argued explicitly:**
1. **Schedule mechanism**: a small, explicit list of `{step, tenant_index, new_sensitivity_class}` dict entries in `migration_schedule` (a top-level config key already present, empty, in every config since before this session -- `configs/default.yaml` had scaffolded it with a stale `{tenant_cohort, new_floor}` comment describing a mechanism that was never built; the real, shipped schema names the field that actually changes -- `sensitivity_class` -- not the floor itself, since the floor is never touched directly, matching PLAN.md's own "scripted, exogenous" framing for S6 vs. S5's adversarial framing). `env/environment.py` gained `migration_graph_seed` (required only under `scenario: S6`, same fail-fast convention as `ddos.graph_seed`) to build the tenant graph once at construction time, decoupled from `episode_seed` -- same reasoning as S4's `ddos.graph_seed`: `tenant_index` must name a stable tenant regardless of eval seed.
2. **Mechanism, Hard Rule 3 compliant**: `env/request_generator.py::RequestGenerator` gained `set_tenant_sensitivity_class(tenant_id, new_sensitivity_class)` -- writes directly into `_tenant_attrs_by_id[tenant_id]`, the *same* dict object NetworkX stores on the graph node (a live reference, not a copy), so the graph itself updates too by construction. `env/environment.py`'s `reset()` gained an `elif self._scenario == "S6"` branch (parallel to S4's) that, unlike S4, keeps a reference to the live `RequestGenerator` instance (`self._request_generator`, not just `iter(...)`'s discarded wrapper) since S6 needs to call a method on it mid-episode. `_advance_to_next_decision`'s tick loop gained one small, unconditional dispatch loop (`for event in self._migration_schedule: if event["step"] == self._step_count: ...`) right after `self._step_count += 1` -- unconditional because `self._migration_schedule` is `[]` for every scenario but S6 (set in `__init__`), so the loop body is a guaranteed no-op elsewhere; no additional `if self._scenario == "S6"` guard needed at the dispatch site. A ratcheted request is a completely ordinary `Request` -- masking.py, the reward calculation, and state construction never learn a ratchet happened (verified at the code level, see Testing).
3. **Hard Rule 8 guard**: `config["train_eligible"]` (`.get(..., True)` -- every existing config, none of which sets this key, is silently unaffected) checked at the very top of `experiments/train.py::train()`, before any env/agent construction, raising `ValueError` if `False`. `configs/scenarios/s6_migration.yaml` is the one config that sets `train_eligible: false`. Deliberately keyed on the flag itself, not a hardcoded `scenario == "S6"` string check -- this stays correct even if a future session fixes `train()`'s hardcoded `"S1"` override (the flagged debt above) to thread `full_config["scenario"]` through; a string-keyed guard would otherwise be silently defeated by that unrelated future fix, while the flag-keyed one wouldn't. This is believed sufficient because it's the one, unavoidable choke point every training campaign in this repo goes through (`train()` is the only training entry point that exists, confirmed by grep) and it fails loudly (raises, doesn't warn) before any real work -- not just documentation.
4. **Schedule chosen for `configs/scenarios/s6_migration.yaml`**: three phased ratchet events (not one) across a 250-step episode, targeting three tenants starting from three *different* real, verified pre-migration classes (S1, S2, S0 -- via a real `build_tenant_graph(seed=0, n_nodes=10)` call, not guessed) so the schedule actually exercises "policy robustness under non-stationarity" rather than one single step change: `step 60: tenant_0 S1->S3`, `step 130: tenant_3 S2->S3`, `step 190: tenant_4 S0->S2`. No event targets an already-S3 tenant (would be a silent no-op).

**Testing (real, request-by-request checks, not smoke tests or aggregate stats):**
- Full regression: all 487 pre-existing tests pass unmodified (S1-S4 dispatch byte-for-byte untouched).
- `tests/test_request_generator.py` (+5): `set_tenant_sensitivity_class` rejects an unknown tenant / an invalid class value (Hard Rule 4); changes subsequent requests starting with the very next one (real before/after request-by-request check on a real, high-traffic tenant -- `tenant_5`, real S0, verified `traffic_rate` under graph_seed 0); leaves every other tenant's `sensitivity_class` byte-for-byte untouched; the underlying graph node reflects the same mutation (single write, not two out-of-sync copies).
- `tests/test_environment.py` (+6, note: `test_s4_and_s6_scenarios_are_not_yet_dispatched` renamed/narrowed to `test_s5_scenario_is_not_yet_dispatched`, S6 removed from its loop since it's now real): `SmartKeyNetEnv(scenario=S6)` without `migration_graph_seed` raises `KeyError`; a direct, request-by-request check through the *real environment* (not just the generator in isolation) confirms every decision for the ratcheted tenant carries `sensitivity_class=0` (S0) strictly before the scripted step and `=3` (S3) at/after it, over a fixed seed, real evidence on both sides (not vacuous); the resulting `policy_floor` changes correspondingly, cross-checked against a **real** `PolicyTable().floor(SensitivityClass.S0/S3, ThreatPosture.CALM)` call (`use_foresight: off` pins posture at CALM so the floor is driven purely by sensitivity_class) -- not a hardcoded expected value; every *other* tenant's `sensitivity_class` stays at its real, graph-sampled value for the whole episode (isolation check, real evidence some other tenant was actually decided); a code-level Hard Rule 3 grep (masking.py/contracts.py contain zero "migration"/"s6" mentions, `_apply_action`'s body contains zero such mentions); a held-out-eval sanity run via `experiments/harness.py::run_scenario` against the real, committed `s6_migration.yaml` with a `StaticThresholdPolicy` baseline -- runs to completion, `floor_violations == 0` holds across the ratchet points too.
- `tests/test_train.py` (+3, **the single most important test this session produces**): attempting `train()` on the real, committed `configs/scenarios/s6_migration.yaml` raises `ValueError` mentioning `train_eligible`, confirmed to fire before any real training work (fast test); a synthetic config proves the guard is keyed on the `train_eligible` flag itself, not a hardcoded `scenario == "S6"` string match; every pre-existing config (`default.yaml`, S2/S3/S4) is confirmed to default `train_eligible` to `True` (no-op for all of them).
- **Total: 501 passed, 1 xfailed** (up from 487 passed, 1 xfailed).

**Hard Rule 3 code-level check (explicit confirmation, not assumed):** grepped the full repo for "s6"/"migration"/"migration_schedule"/"migration_graph_seed"/"train_eligible" across all `*.py` files -- hits are exactly: `env/environment.py`/`env/request_generator.py` (the sanctioned dispatch/mechanism), `experiments/train.py` (the Hard Rule 8 guard), the new/updated test files, and `experiments/harness.py`/`tests/test_harness.py`'s pre-existing, scenario-agnostic "S1-S6" docstring prose (harness genuinely runs any policy against any scenario by design -- not new branching). `env/masking.py`, `env/contracts.py`, and `SmartKeyNetEnv._apply_action`'s body are completely clean.

**Held-out-eval sanity run finding:** under `configs/scenarios/s6_migration.yaml`'s real schedule, a grid-reasonable `StaticThresholdPolicy(pool_fill_threshold=0.5)` runs a full S6 episode to completion with `floor_violations == 0` across all three ratchet points (`step 60/130/190`) -- confirms the scenario is genuinely usable for its stated purpose (held-out evaluation), not a rigorous benchmark (no DQN-vs-baseline comparison was run or is appropriate here, since Hard Rule 8 forbids training on this scenario at all).

**What got done:**
- `env/request_generator.py`: `RequestGenerator.set_tenant_sensitivity_class` (new, additive method).
- `env/environment.py`: design decision 15 -- `migration_graph_seed`/`migration_schedule` config parsing, tenant-graph build extended to S6, `reset()` gained an `elif self._scenario == "S6"` branch keeping a live `RequestGenerator` reference, `_advance_to_next_decision` gained the scripted-ratchet dispatch loop. Module + class docstrings updated.
- `experiments/train.py`: `train()` gained the Hard Rule 8 `train_eligible` guard, checked before any training work.
- `configs/scenarios/s6_migration.yaml` (new file): real, argued 3-event schedule + `train_eligible: false`.
- `configs/default.yaml`: `migration_schedule`'s comment corrected to describe the real, shipped schema (superseding the stale, never-implemented `{tenant_cohort, new_floor}` phrasing) -- comment-only, the key itself stays `[]`.
- `tests/test_request_generator.py` (+5), `tests/test_environment.py` (+6, 1 renamed/narrowed), `tests/test_train.py` (+3).

**What's working:** the full S1-S6 scenario grid is now real and dispatched -- this is a genuine milestone (PLAN.md §5's entire scenario table now has working code behind every row except S5). Hard Rule 8 is enforced in code, not just documented.
**What's broken / incomplete:** none for this session's scope. Standing, unrelated debt (unfixed, flagged a third time now): `experiments/train.py`'s hardcoded `scenario: "S1"` override in `train()` still blocks any real multi-scenario training campaign -- separate from and does not weaken the Hard Rule 8 guard (see Design decision 3's reasoning for why the guard doesn't depend on that debt staying unfixed).
**Blockers:** none.
**Next session will:** per PROGRESS.md's "Next task" -- Dashboard v2's Threat Input panel remains blocked on dataset ingestion; unblocked alternatives (Panel 2, `agents/soft_reward_baseline.py`) remain open; `experiments/train.py`'s hardcoded-S1 debt (now flagged a third time) is a real, standalone future task.
**Hard Rules check:** Hard Rule 8 was the central constraint -- built a real, code-level guard (not documentation) and tested that it fires. Hard Rule 3 -- verified at the code level (grep) that masking.py/contracts.py/the reward calculation never became scenario-aware; the mechanism is entirely upstream in request generation, same standard as S4. Hard Rule 4 -- the three ratcheted classes (S1->S3, S2->S3, S0->S2) are all real `SensitivityClass` enum values, none invented; `set_tenant_sensitivity_class` validates this via the enum constructor. Hard Rules 1/2/9 -- untouched, no reward/masking-table/deferral-logic changes; the ratchet works entirely by changing what `sensitivity_class` a request carries, letting existing floor/masking logic respond exactly as it already does.
